- [ ] **Step 3: Implement the two pure pieces**

```ts
// lib/pal/gdm/onboarding.ts
export type GdmStep = "told" | "not_told" | "date" | "consent";
export type GdmAnswer = "yes" | "not_yet" | "continue" | "skip" | "back";

/** The whole onboarding, as a table. No step takes a lab value or asks about medicine. */
export function nextGdmStep(step: GdmStep, answer: GdmAnswer): GdmStep | "submit" {
  if (step === "told") return answer === "yes" ? "date" : "not_told";
  if (step === "not_told") return "told";
  if (step === "date") return answer === "back" ? "told" : "consent"; // G-27: was `return "consent"`, so "back" went forward
  return answer === "back" ? "date" : "submit";
}
```

```ts
// lib/server/gdm-door.ts
import { eq } from "drizzle-orm";
import { notFound, redirect } from "next/navigation";

import { gdmDoorEnabled, gdmDoorServerEnabled } from "../gdm-door-flag";
import { GDM_ROUTES } from "../pal/gdm/routes";
import { safeDecrypt } from "./crypto";
import { getDb, schema, type Db } from "./db";
import { getSessionInfo, type SessionInfo } from "./session";

export type GdmDoorState =
  | { kind: "closed" }
  | { kind: "signed_out" }
  | { kind: "no_profile"; userId: string }
  | { kind: "open"; userId: string; appointmentDate: string | null };

type Deps = { db?: () => Db; getSession?: () => Promise<SessionInfo> };

export async function gdmDoorState(deps: Deps = {}): Promise<GdmDoorState> {
  if (!(gdmDoorServerEnabled() && gdmDoorEnabled("organiser"))) return { kind: "closed" };
  const session = await (deps.getSession ?? getSessionInfo)();
  if (!session) return { kind: "signed_out" };
  const [row] = await (deps.db ?? getDb)()
    .select({ appointment: schema.gdmProfiles.appointmentCiphertext })
    .from(schema.gdmProfiles)
    .where(eq(schema.gdmProfiles.userId, session.userId));
  if (!row) return { kind: "no_profile", userId: session.userId };
  return { kind: "open", userId: session.userId, appointmentDate: row.appointment ? safeDecrypt(row.appointment) : null };
}

/** Call first in every page under app/gdm/(door)/. notFound()/redirect() throw, so this only returns when open. */
export async function requireGdmDoor(): Promise<{ userId: string; appointmentDate: string | null }> {
  const state = await gdmDoorState();
  if (state.kind === "closed") notFound();
  if (state.kind === "signed_out") redirect(GDM_ROUTES.signup);
  if (state.kind === "no_profile") redirect(GDM_ROUTES.start);
  return { userId: state.userId, appointmentDate: state.appointmentDate };
}
```

}

Erase and Delete my account call `clearGdmDeviceKeys()` too, after their 200. Shared routes, no new server code. `clearGdmDeviceKeys` collects the matching keys first and removes them after — removing while iterating `storage.key(i)` skips entries.

The five `gdm-privacy-notice` strings as a heading and four paragraphs. The frame's footer link to it (`app/gdm/layout.tsx`) renders under the same condition. **Counsel reads this row before `organiser` goes live** (§1).

The general answer to eight of the twelve rows, the smallest copy set, a plain list. **No model call on this path. ##### Task 2.1: `/api/gdm/items` — stored with consent, encrypted, clinical routes first

> **Review amendments (read before starting).**
> - **G-12 (CRITICAL GAP, applied below):** a row that will not decrypt is counted and reported, never silently dropped.
> - **G-16:** do **not** copy the route helpers. Create `lib/server/gdm-route.ts` with `gdmRouteGuard(deps, options?: { consent?: boolean }): Promise<{ userId: string } | Response>`: closed → 404, no session → 401, and unless `consent: false`, no `gdm_profiles` row → 403 with `gdm-consent-required.line`. About twenty lines, tested once (`tests/unit/pal/gdm-route-guard.test.ts`); each route keeps its own three status assertions. "Two files do not earn a shared module" was written when it was two; with the photo route and Tier 2's read route it is four copies of a security-critical order.
> - **G-11:** in `POST`, compute `route` and `routeCopy` **before** the insert, and call `loadSafetyContract()` on **every** ask, not only when a route fires. As first written the contract is read for the first time on the rarest and most serious input the list will ever see, *after* the row is stored: a missing or malformed ledger would answer a woman who types that she is shaky with "That did not save just now", save her question anyway, and duplicate it on retry. Add a case with an injected contract loader that throws: the response is a 500 and `gdm_items` is empty.
> - **G-13:** Replace is **one server operation**, not two client calls. `POST` accepts an optional `replaces: <uuid>`, valid only with `kind: "plan"` (400 otherwise). In one transaction: load the old row by `id`, `userId` and `kind = 'plan'` (404 if absent, 409 if its body already has a `replacedOn`), insert the new plan, then rewrite the old body with `replacedOn` set to the new plan's `enteredOn`. Cases: after a replace there is exactly one current plan and the old one is dated; another user's id is a 404 and nothing is inserted; `replaces` with `kind: "ask"` is a 400. The two-call version could leave two current plans with no control anywhere that retires one.
> - **G-14:** a 409 gets its own row, `gdm-list-full` — `line`: This list is full. Delete an entry to add another. "Try again in a moment" is untrue for a full list.
> - **G-21:** name the helper `askListCardRoute`, not `routeAboveFifth`: what it answers, not how. Add a case to the test below: another user's `PATCH` is refused (only `DELETE` is tried today).

- Create: `lib/pal/gdm/items.ts`, `lib/pal/gdm/asklist.ts`, `lib/server/gdm-items.ts`, `app/api/gdm/items/route.ts`

**Interfaces:**

  - Route: `GET ?kind=` → `{ items }` · `POST { kind, body }` → `{ id, route: ClinicalRoute | null, routeCopy: string | null }` · `PATCH { id, body }` → `{ ok: true }` · `DELETE ?id=` → `{ ok: true }`. 404 closed · 401 signed out · 403 no consented GDM profile · 400 invalid · 409 over the cap.

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

```ts
// lib/pal/gdm/items.ts
import { z } from "zod";

/** What she can keep. A photo of her sheet has its own route (Task 3.3). */
export const GDM_ITEM_KINDS = ["ask", "plan", "meal"] as const;
export type GdmItemKind = (typeof GDM_ITEM_KINDS)[number];

/** Her own words only. Strict: there is no field for a reading, a dose or a medicine, and none can be slipped in. */
export const AskBodySchema = z
  .object({
    text: z.string().trim().min(1).max(500),
    note: z.string().trim().min(1).max(500).nullable(),
    asked: z.boolean(),
    answer: z.string().trim().min(1).max(1000).nullable()
  })
  .strict();
export type AskBody = z.infer<typeof AskBodySchema>;

/** Per-kind body schema. A kind with no schema yet is refused by the route. */
export const GDM_ITEM_BODY: Partial<Record<GdmItemKind, z.ZodType>> = { ask: AskBodySchema };

// ponytail: a flat per-kind cap instead of rate limiting — these routes make no paid call.
export const GDM_ITEM_CAP = 200;
```

```ts
// lib/pal/gdm/asklist.ts
import { CLINICAL_ROUTES, classifyClinicalRisk, type ClinicalRoute } from "../clinical-risk";

// The routes PRD §6.2 names for the list: "urgent symptoms, possible
// hypoglycaemia, medication dosing and eating-disorder language are answered
// by their own approved rows first." They are the first four of
// CLINICAL_ROUTES — tests/unit/pal/gdm-pregnancy-route.test.ts pins that order.
// classifyClinicalRisk returns the FIRST match in precedence order, so when one
// of these four matches it is always the one returned. Every lower route gets
// no card here: nothing is read behind this list, and the fixed line above it
// already names the care team (§8 item 8 asks the safety owner to confirm).
const FIRST_FOUR: readonly ClinicalRoute[] = CLINICAL_ROUTES.slice(0, 4);

export function routeAboveFifth(text: string): ClinicalRoute | null {
  const hit = classifyClinicalRisk(text);
  return hit && FIRST_FOUR.includes(hit.route) ? hit.route : null;
}
```

`lib/server/gdm-items.ts` — `listGdmItems` selects `id, bodyCiphertext, createdAt, updatedAt` where `userId` and `kind` match, `orderBy(desc(createdAt))`, maps each row through `safeDecrypt` + `JSON.parse` in a `try`, and leaves out rows that do not parse (they still export, as the placeholder — Task 1.3). **A row left out must reach somebody (G-12).** As first written this was a swallowed `catch`: an entry of hers could vanish, still count toward her cap, and neither she nor the owner would ever know. `listGdmItems` takes an optional fifth argument, `report: (event: { kind: GdmItemKind; unreadable: number }) => void`, whose default sends one Sentry message through the server's existing allowlisted client: the kind and a count, **never an id, never content**. It is called once per list, and only when the count is above zero. Add two cases to this task's test, with an injected `report`: a row whose ciphertext is `v1:x` is left out of the result and reported as `{ kind: "ask", unreadable: 1 }`; a clean list reports nothing.

`app/api/gdm/items/route.ts` — `createGdmItemsHandlers(deps)`, same `Deps` as the profile route. **The guard is shared, not copied (G-16):** every method starts with `const gate = await gdmRouteGuard(deps); if (gate instanceof Response) return gate;` from `lib/server/gdm-route.ts`, which holds the order in one tested place: `closed()` → 404 · session → 401 · **consent gate** (select `gdmProfiles.consentedAt` for the user; none → 403 with `GDM_COPY["gdm-consent-required"].line`). Then parse. `POST`: `z.object({ kind: z.enum(GDM_ITEM_KINDS), body: z.unknown() }).strict()`, then `GDM_ITEM_BODY[kind]?.safeParse(body)` (no schema or failure → 400); count rows of that kind for the user (≥ `GDM_ITEM_CAP` → 409 with `gdm-list-full.line`). **Then, before anything is stored (G-11):** for `kind === "ask"`, `const contract = loadSafetyContract()` on every ask, `route = routeAboveFifth(\`${text} ${note ?? ""}\`)` and `routeCopy = route ? contract.copy.clinicalRoutes[route] : null`. A contract that cannot be read fails the request here, loudly, on the first ordinary question, with nothing saved and a retry that is safe. Only then insert `{ userId, kind, bodyCiphertext: encryptField(JSON.stringify(parsed)) }` returning `id`; with `replaces` set, the insert and the dating of the old plan are one transaction (G-13). `PATCH`: `{ id: z.string().uuid(), body }`; look the row up **by `id` and `userId`**, validate the body with the stored row's kind, update `bodyCiphertext` and `updatedAt`; zero rows → 404. `DELETE`: `?id=` uuid, delete by `id` and `userId`; zero rows → 404. `export const runtime = "nodejs";`.

// tests/unit/pal/gdm-plan-record.test.ts
import fs from "node:fs";
import path from "node:path";

import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";

import { currentPlans, detectConflicts, GdmPlanSchema, type StoredPlan } from "../../../lib/pal/gdm/plan-record";

const plan = (id: string, over: Partial<StoredPlan> = {}): StoredPlan => ({
  id,
  givenBy: null,
  note: null,
  perDay: null,
  unit: "grams",
  choiceMeans: null,
  figures: {},
  enteredOn: "2026-10-01",
  replacedOn: null,
  ...over
});

describe("plan record (PRD §6.2 F-PLANKEEP; §7.4 deep module 1)", () => {
  it("an empty plan is valid and stays empty — no default is ever supplied", () => {
    const parsed = GdmPlanSchema.parse({
      givenBy: null, note: null, perDay: null, unit: "none", choiceMeans: null, figures: {}, enteredOn: "2026-10-01", replacedOn: null
    });
    expect(parsed.figures).toEqual({});
    expect(detectConflicts([])).toEqual([]);
  });

  it("keeps her figure exactly as written — a string, in her clinic's unit", () => {
    const parsed = GdmPlanSchema.parse(plan("x", { unit: "choices", choiceMeans: "15 g", figures: { lunch: "3 to 4 choices" } }));
    expect(parsed.figures.lunch).toBe("3 to 4 choices");
  });

  it("refuses an unknown unit, an unknown occasion and any extra key", () => {
    expect(GdmPlanSchema.safeParse({ ...plan("x"), unit: "exchanges" }).success).toBe(false);
    expect(GdmPlanSchema.safeParse({ ...plan("x"), figures: { brunch: "30" } }).success).toBe(false);
    expect(GdmPlanSchema.safeParse({ ...plan("x"), target: "under 140" }).success).toBe(false);
  });

  it("two current entries that differ for an occasion are flagged — and neither is picked", () => {
    const a = plan("a", { givenBy: "the dietitian", figures: { lunch: "45 g", dinner: "45 g" } });
    const b = plan("b", { givenBy: "the clinic nurse", figures: { lunch: "30 g", dinner: "45  G" } });
    expect(detectConflicts([a, b])).toEqual([{ occasion: "lunch", planIds: ["a", "b"] }]);
  });

  it("the same digits in two units differ: nothing is ever converted", () => {
    const a = plan("a", { unit: "choices", choiceMeans: "15 g", figures: { breakfast: "3" } });
    const b = plan("b", { unit: "servings", figures: { breakfast: "3" } });
    expect(detectConflicts([a, b])).toHaveLength(1);
  });

  it("a replaced plan is kept, dated, and takes no part in conflicts", () => {
    const old = plan("old", { figures: { lunch: "30 g" }, replacedOn: "2026-10-20" });
    const now = plan("now", { figures: { lunch: "45 g" }, enteredOn: "2026-10-20" });
    expect(currentPlans([old, now]).map((p) => p.id)).toEqual(["now"]);
    expect(detectConflicts([old, now])).toEqual([]);
  });

  it("the module does no arithmetic on a figure", () => {
    const source = fs.readFileSync(path.join(process.cwd(), "lib/pal/gdm/plan-record.ts"), "utf8");
    expect(source).not.toMatch(/parseFloat|parseInt|Number\(|Math\./);

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement** — append to `lib/pal/gdm/plan-record.ts`:

```ts
import { z } from "zod";

const IsoDate = z.string().regex(/^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$/);
const words = (max: number) => z.string().trim().min(1).max(max);

/**
 * Her plan, in her clinician's words (PRD §6.2 F-PLANKEEP). Every figure is a
 * STRING, exactly as written for her: nothing here parses, converts, compares
 * by size, defaults, or judges one. Strict, so a field for a target, a reading
 * or a medicine cannot be added without editing this schema.
 */
export const GdmPlanSchema = z
  .object({
    givenBy: words(80).nullable(),
    note: words(2000).nullable(),
    perDay: words(60).nullable(),
    unit: z.enum(GDM_UNITS),
    choiceMeans: words(40).nullable(),
    figures: z.partialRecord(z.enum(GDM_OCCASIONS), words(40)),
    enteredOn: IsoDate,
    replacedOn: IsoDate.nullable()
  })
  .strict();
export type GdmPlan = z.infer<typeof GdmPlanSchema>;
export type StoredPlan = GdmPlan & { id: string };

export function currentPlans(plans: StoredPlan[]): StoredPlan[] {
  return plans.filter((plan) => plan.replacedOn === null);
}

export type PlanConflict = { occasion: GdmOccasion; planIds: [string, string] };

const asWritten = (text: string) => text.trim().replace(/\s+/g, " ").toLowerCase();

/** Pairs of CURRENT entries that say different things for one occasion. Shown side by side; never resolved. */
export function detectConflicts(plans: StoredPlan[]): PlanConflict[] {
  const current = currentPlans(plans);
  const conflicts: PlanConflict[] = [];
  for (const occasion of GDM_OCCASIONS) {
    const holders = current.filter((plan) => plan.figures[occasion] !== undefined);
    for (let i = 0; i < holders.length; i += 1) {
      for (let j = i + 1; j < holders.length; j += 1) {
        const [a, b] = [holders[i], holders[j]];
        const same = a.unit === b.unit && asWritten(a.figures[occasion]!) === asWritten(b.figures[occasion]!);
        if (!same) conflicts.push({ occasion, planIds: [a.id, b.id] });
      }
    }
  }
  return conflicts;
}
```

(`z.partialRecord` is zod 4's non-exhaustive enum-keyed record; `z.record(z.enum(…), …)` is exhaustive in zod 4 and would demand all six occasions.)

>
> ```ts
> // lib/client/gdm-date.ts
> /** Today as YYYY-MM-DD in the DEVICE's timezone. Never toISOString(): that is UTC, and west of Greenwich it becomes tomorrow every evening. */
> export function localIsoDate(now: Date = new Date()): string {
>   const pad = (n: number) => String(n).padStart(2, "0");
>   return `${now.getFullYear()}-${pad(now.getMonth() + 1)}-${pad(now.getDate())}`;
> }
>
> /** Her date, for display. Built from (y, m - 1, d), which is LOCAL midnight. `new Date("2026-10-08")` is UTC midnight and shows as 7 October across the Americas. */
> export function formatIsoDate(iso: string | null, locale?: string): string | null {
>   const m = /^(\d{4})-(\d{2})-(\d{2})$/.exec(iso ?? "");
>   if (!m) return null;
>   const date = new Date(Number(m[1]), Number(m[2]) - 1, Number(m[3]));
>   return new Intl.DateTimeFormat(locale, { weekday: "long", day: "numeric", month: "long" }).format(date);
> }
> ```
>

**Rows filed (surface `organiser`):**

| Copy ID | Named strings |
|---|---|
| `gdm-occasions` | `breakfast`: Breakfast · `snack_morning`: Morning snack · `lunch`: Lunch · `snack_afternoon`: Afternoon snack · `dinner`: Dinner · `snack_bedtime`: Bedtime snack |
| `gdm-plan-controls` | `title`: My plan · `sub`: In your care team's words. · `givenBy`: Who gave you this · `note`: What the sheet says · `perDay`: Meals and snacks per day, as written · `counts`: How my clinic counts · `grams`: Grams · `choices`: Choices · `servings`: Servings · `none`: None given · `choiceMeans`: One choice is, as your sheet says · `figure`: Figure for this occasion, exactly as written · `save`: Save · `replace`: Replace this plan · `replacedOn`: Replaced on · `enteredOn`: Entered on · `photoAdd`: Add a photo of your sheet · `photoRemove`: Remove photo · `empty`: Nothing here yet. |
| `gdm-plan-differ` | `flag`: These differ · `park`: Add to my questions · `askText`: Two entries in my plan differ for {occasion}. Which one stands? |

The label is "How my clinic counts", not "Unit": `unit` is in the copy test's medication family (a dose is counted in units), and the bank should not need an exemption. The bedtime-snack label is a heading, never advice (row 11). `askText` is the only product-authored sentence that lands in her list; it states that two entries differ and asks which stands — no clinician is named, no entry is preferred (row 12).

##### Task 3.4: F-CALM-GDM audit — PR-3

- [ ] `npm run test:pal -- gdm-copy gdm-plan-keep gdm-plan-record`. Expected: PASS.
- [ ] §7.1 checklist. #### PR-4 — F-WAIT: waiting-for-my-dietitian mode

##### Task 4.1: When waiting mode shows, and how the date is spoken

**Files:**
- Create: `lib/pal/gdm/waiting.ts`
- Test: `tests/unit/pal/gdm-waiting.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Produces: `waitingState({ hasCurrentPlan, appointmentDate, today }): "waiting" | "after_appointment" | "ended"`; `appointmentPhrase(appointmentDate: string | null, today: string): "today" | "tomorrow" | "thisWeek" | "nextWeek" | "later" | "none"`; `summaryOfferDue(appointmentDate, today): boolean`. Dates are `YYYY-MM-DD` strings; `today` is passed in (device-local), never read from a clock inside the module.

The countdown is spoken in **words from the bank, never a number of days**: a day count is a digit she did not type (PRD §8 rule 5), and F-CALM-GDM forbids a countdown framed as pressure.

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

```ts
// lib/pal/gdm/waiting.ts
const DAY_MS = 86_400_000;

/** Whole days from `today` to `date`; null when either is not a real YYYY-MM-DD. */
function daysUntil(date: string | null, today: string): number | null {
  const parse = (text: string | null) => {
    const match = /^(\d{4})-(\d{2})-(\d{2})$/.exec(text ?? "");
    return match ? Date.UTC(Number(match[1]), Number(match[2]) - 1, Number(match[3])) : null;
  };
  const [then, now] = [parse(date), parse(today)];
  return then === null || now === null ? null : Math.round((then - now) / DAY_MS);
}

export type WaitingState = "waiting" | "after_appointment" | "ended";

export function waitingState(input: { hasCurrentPlan: boolean; appointmentDate: string | null; today: string }): WaitingState {
  if (input.hasCurrentPlan) return "ended";
  const days = daysUntil(input.appointmentDate, input.today);
  return days !== null && days < 0 ? "after_appointment" : "waiting";
}

export type AppointmentPhrase = "today" | "tomorrow" | "thisWeek" | "nextWeek" | "later" | "none";

export function appointmentPhrase(appointmentDate: string | null, today: string): AppointmentPhrase {
  const days = daysUntil(appointmentDate, today);
  if (days === null || days < 0) return "none";
  if (days === 0) return "today";
  if (days === 1) return "tomorrow";
  if (days <= 6) return "thisWeek";
  if (days <= 13) return "nextWeek";
  return "later";
}

export function summaryOfferDue(appointmentDate: string | null, today: string): boolean {
  const days = daysUntil(appointmentDate, today);
  return days === 0 || days === 1;
}
```

##### Task 4.2: Home — the waiting screen, or her plan once it is entered

> **Review amendments (read before starting). G-32 and G-37 change what this task builds; both are applied here and override Step 3's `ended` description below.**
>
> **G-32 (HIGH, owner decision): the appointment block is a fixture of Home, in all three states.** As first written, the appointment phrase, the control that changes the date, and the "your summary is ready to print" line lived only in the waiting states. A woman who types in her obstetrician's handout *before* she has seen the dietitian, which is PRD row 12's own scenario, would lose the countdown, could no longer correct her date anywhere on the door (nothing else calls `PATCH /api/gdm/profile`), and would never be offered the summary she is now best placed to print. The plan followed PRD §6.2 to the letter ("the mode ends on its own when a plan is entered"); the owner has ruled that what ends is the **waiting content**, not the appointment.
>
> **G-37: PRD story 4 says "my plan replaces the waiting screen". It must be her plan, not a link to it.** From the second week on, the reason she opens this door at a mealtime is to see what her sheet says for that occasion.
>
> Add to `lib/pal/gdm/waiting.ts`, with cases in `gdm-waiting.test.ts`:
>
> ```ts
> /** What Home renders in each state. The appointment block is not here on purpose: it is ALWAYS rendered (G-32). */
> export function homeParts(state: WaitingState): { structure: boolean; checklist: boolean; prompt: boolean; plan: boolean } {
>   if (state === "ended") return { structure: false, checklist: false, prompt: false, plan: true };
>   return { structure: true, checklist: true, prompt: state === "after_appointment", plan: false };
> }
> ```
>
> ```ts
>   it("when a plan arrives the waiting content ends, and her plan takes its place (G-32, G-37)", () => {
>     expect(homeParts("waiting")).toEqual({ structure: true, checklist: true, prompt: false, plan: false });
>     expect(homeParts("after_appointment")).toMatchObject({ prompt: true, plan: false });
>     expect(homeParts("ended")).toEqual({ structure: false, checklist: false, prompt: false, plan: true });
>   });
> ```
>
> **G-69 (owner, 2026-09-20): Home leads with her appointment, in every state.** The PRD lists waiting mode's four parts as (a) ACOG's line, (b) the checklist, (c) the appointment, (d) the links; that is a list, not a layout. Her date and her next action lead. ACOG's quoted sentence is the riskiest content on the door and is still open under D5, so a screen built around it collapses if it is refused; and a fixture (G-32) belongs in one fixed place.
>
> On screen, top to bottom. **`waiting`:** the appointment block (c) · the checklist (b) · ACOG's line, quoted and attributed (a) · the links (d). **`after_appointment`:** the appointment block, then the one prompt and "Open My plan", then the rest. **`ended`:** the appointment block while a date is ahead, with the summary offer when `summaryOfferDue`; then her current plan or plans, read-only, the same card the plan page renders, conflicts flagged (no new copy: the occasion and field labels exist); then the links. If the safety owner refuses the ACOG row, part (a) is simply absent and nothing else moves. The server page passes the current plans to the component as well as `hasCurrentPlan`.
> - **G-44:** `today` is `localIsoDate()` and her date is shown with `formatIsoDate()`, both from `lib/client/gdm-date.ts` (Task 3.2). Never `new Date(appointmentDate)` and never `toISOString()`: in Los Angeles those show "Wednesday, October 7" for an appointment on the 8th, and turn "tomorrow" into "today" every evening. Verified by running both.
> - **G-24, a note for the safety owner:** ACOG's sentence ends with a full stop and the pinned `quote` does not. If the row is to be called verbatim, keep the full stop (it was checked against `blind/web/sheet_acog.txt`, line 458).

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement** — in `lib/pal/gdm/items.ts`:

```ts
export const MealBodySchema = z
  .object({ occasion: z.enum(GDM_OCCASIONS), text: z.string().trim().min(1).max(200), inSummary: z.boolean() })
  .strict();
export type MealBody = z.infer<typeof MealBodySchema>;

export function groupMeals<T extends { body: MealBody }>(meals: T[]): Record<GdmOccasion, T[]> {
  return Object.fromEntries(
    GDM_OCCASIONS.map((occasion) => [occasion, meals.filter((meal) => meal.body.occasion === occasion)])
  ) as Record<GdmOccasion, T[]>;
}
```

and register `meal: MealBodySchema`. The page calls `requireGdmDoor()`; the component renders **one add form** (G-70: the `field` textarea, a native `<select>` of the six occasions labelled by `gdm-meals-controls.occasion`, and `save`), then an `<h2>` from `gdm-occasions` for **each occasion that holds at least one meal** (in `GDM_OCCASIONS` order, the bedtime snack like any other), each meal with the `inSummary` checkbox (story 3: "copy a day's meals into my appointment summary") and `remove`. On a 200: `track({ name: "gdm_meal_saved", props: { occasion } })`. **Task 5.1's fourth test goes red the day someone adds a read or an idea here without going through Task G3.4 — that is what it is for; G3.4 amends it.**

// tests/unit/pal/gdm-summary.test.ts
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";

import { buildSummary, type SummaryLabels } from "../../../lib/pal/gdm/summary";

const labels: SummaryLabels = {
  title: "T", plan: "P", meals: "M", asks: "A", empty: "E", differ: "D",
  occasions: { breakfast: "Ob", snack_morning: "Om", lunch: "Ol", snack_afternoon: "Oa", dinner: "Od", snack_bedtime: "Os" },
  units: { grams: "Ug", choices: "Uc", servings: "Us", none: "Un" },
  fields: { givenBy: "Fg", perDay: "Fp", counts: "Fc", choiceMeans: "Fm", enteredOn: "Fe" }
};
const USER = ["the dietitian", "three meals two snacks", "45 g", "30 g", "dal and rice", "why was it higher", "after the same dinner", "2026-10-01"];
const input = {
  plans: [
    { id: "a", givenBy: "the dietitian", note: null, perDay: "three meals two snacks", unit: "grams" as const, choiceMeans: null, figures: { lunch: "45 g" }, enteredOn: "2026-10-01", replacedOn: null },
    { id: "b", givenBy: null, note: null, perDay: null, unit: "grams" as const, choiceMeans: null, figures: { lunch: "30 g" }, enteredOn: "2026-10-01", replacedOn: null }
  ],
  meals: [
    { body: { occasion: "lunch" as const, text: "dal and rice", inSummary: true } },
    { body: { occasion: "dinner" as const, text: "not for the page", inSummary: false } }
  ],
  asks: [
    { body: { text: "why was it higher", note: "after the same dinner", asked: false, answer: null } },
    { body: { text: "already asked", note: null, asked: true, answer: "fine" } }
  ]
};

describe("summary builder (PRD §6.2 F-SUMMARY; §7.4 deep module 2)", () => {
  it("three sections, fixed order, fixed headings — and no readings section exists", () => {
    const doc = buildSummary(input, labels);
    expect(doc.sections.map((s) => s.key)).toEqual(["plan", "meals", "asks"]);
    expect(doc.sections.map((s) => s.heading)).toEqual(["P", "M", "A"]);
  });

  it("contains only her text and fixed labels: strip both and nothing but punctuation is left", () => {
    const doc = buildSummary(input, labels);
    const fixed = [labels.differ, ...Object.values(labels.occasions), ...Object.values(labels.units), ...Object.values(labels.fields)];
    for (const line of doc.sections.flatMap((s) => s.lines)) {
      let rest = line;
      for (const piece of [...USER, ...fixed].sort((a, b) => b.length - a.length)) rest = rest.split(piece).join("");
      expect(rest, line).toMatch(/^[\s:·—-]*$/);
    }
  });

  it("carries only the meals she marked, only her open questions, and shows where two entries differ without choosing", () => {
    const text = JSON.stringify(buildSummary(input, labels));
    expect(text).toContain("dal and rice");
    expect(text).not.toContain("not for the page");
    expect(text).toContain("why was it higher");
    expect(text).not.toContain("already asked");
    expect(text).toContain("45 g");
    expect(text).toContain("30 g");
    expect(text).toContain("D");
  });

  it("an empty organiser builds three empty sections", () => {

    expect(doc.sections.every((s) => s.lines.length === 0)).toBe(true);
    expect(doc.emptyLine).toBe("E");
  });
});
```

const analytics = fs.readFileSync("lib/client/analytics.ts", "utf8");

  });

    const tables = ["gdm_profiles", "gdm_items"];
    const events = named.filter((name) => !tables.includes(name));
    expect(events.length).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(6);

    const next = section.indexOf("### 14.", start + heading.length);
    return section.slice(start, next === -1 ? undefined : next);

- [ ] **Step 3: Write §14** — append to `docs/ops/launch-controls.md`, verbatim:

```markdown
## 14. The GDM door's reads (PRD GDM v1.1 §9.1, §9.2)

One place to read the GDM door's numbers from. Every event named here is a typed
event in `lib/client/analytics.ts` (closed enums only; the no-PII source scan
enforces it). Umami is cookieless and carries no identity; nothing here joins the
GDM profile to a person. **Nothing here measures glucose, weight, medication,
delivery or any pregnancy outcome, and nothing will.** Retention is not a goal:
this audience leaves by design within months.

All thresholds are **[A]** — chosen without a baseline. The owner rules on a fired
line; nothing reverts automatically.

**Sample floor for every line: 50 door users.** A door user is a `gdm_profiles`
row (exact): `select count(*) from gdm_profiles where created_at >= :live;`
Analytics proxy: unique visitors with `gdm_onboarding_step` where `step = done`.
Below 50 the line is unread — it has neither fired nor been survived.

**The clock** starts the day the `organiser` surface is live in production
(`GET /api/health` → `gdmDoor.organiser = "on"`), not the day the code merged.

### 14.1 KL-T1 — is the door used as an organiser? (four weeks after `organiser` is live)

Fires when **A < 25% AND B < 25%**, read at 50 door users or more.

- **A — parked anything.** Numerator: unique visitors with `gdm_ask_parked`.
  Denominator: unique visitors with `gdm_asklist_opened`.
- **B — entered a plan or saved a meal.** Numerator: unique visitors with
  `gdm_plan_entered` or `gdm_meal_saved`. Denominator: door users
  (`gdm_onboarding_step`, `step = done`).
- **Exact cross-check for B and for A's numerator** (coarse plaintext columns
  only; nothing is decrypted):
  `select kind, count(distinct user_id) from gdm_items where created_at >= :live group by kind;`
  and `select count(distinct user_id) from gdm_items where kind in ('plan','meal') and created_at >= :live;`
  each over `select count(*) from gdm_profiles where created_at >= :live;`.
  If Umami and the database disagree on B by more than ten points, assume
  analytics is blind (the 2026-07-22 CSP blackout precedent) and read the database.
- **Fires ⇒** Tier 2 work pauses and the door is re-read (PRD §9.2).
  **Revert lever:** unset `GDM_DOOR_ENABLED` and redeploy. The production guard
  treats a missing twin as a closed door and never fails that build, so this one
  variable darkens every GDM surface. Unset `NEXT_PUBLIC_GDM_DOOR` afterwards.
  Stored data stays exportable and erasable with the door closed.
- **The exact floor for A.** Everyone who opened the list is a door user, so
  (distinct users with an `ask` row) over (door users) can only *understate* A.
  If that exact figure is 25% or more, A has not fired, whatever Umami says.
  Umami's visitor is a hash that includes the IP, so one phone moving between
  Wi-Fi and mobile data is several visitors, most of whom open the list without
  parking: the Umami ratio reads low. Use it only when the exact floor is under 25%.
- **Unread is a finding too.** If a line is still unread eight weeks after
  `organiser` went live, fewer than 50 door users arrived. That is a distribution
  result, not a neutral one: no Tier 2 module (plan §5) starts until KL-T1 has
  been *read*, and the owner re-reads the first-cohort source row in the plan's §1.
- **Before reading any line as "nobody used it":** check Sentry for `/api/gdm/`
  errors, and check the funnel in §14.4. A sign-up that never reaches onboarding
  is more often a lost magic link than a lost interest.

**Umami view:** Events → `gdm_ask_parked`, `gdm_asklist_opened`, `gdm_plan_entered`,
`gdm_meal_saved`, `gdm_onboarding_step` (filter `step = done`), unique visitors,
date range = the four weeks.

### 14.2 D7 — the spending trigger

KL-T1 read at 50 door users or more **and not fired** = "the four-week Tier 1
line is survived". Numerator and Denominator: KL-T1's. Until then: no domain, no
store listing, no trademark work (PRD §3). A line left unread because fewer
than 50 door users arrived is **not** survival.

### 14.3 KL-D1 — does anyone hold a figure? (D1(a)'s own line)

Fires when **fewer than 25%** of door users have entered a figure, read at 50
door users or more, four weeks after `organiser` is live.

- Numerator: unique visitors with `gdm_plan_entered` where `has_figure = true`.
- Denominator: door users (`gdm_onboarding_step`, `step = done`).
- **Fires ⇒** D1 goes back to the owner with the count: F-PLANREAD reaches
  almost nobody. There is no exact cross-check — whether a plan holds a figure
  is inside the encrypted body, on purpose.

**Umami view:** Events → `gdm_plan_entered`, filter `has_figure = true`, unique
visitors, beside the same view unfiltered (the `unit` breakdown is §9.1's
"unit chosen").

### 14.4 The other §9.1 reads (directions, not targets)

`gdm_waiting_opened` in a visitor's first session · `gdm_ask_parked` per active
visitor in week one · `gdm_plan_entered` by `unit` · `gdm_meal_saved` by day
seven, by `occasion` · `gdm_summary_printed` by `before_appointment` ·
`gdm_onboarding_step` where `step = not_told_exit` (counted, not chased) ·
`clinical_route` (which existing route fired; shared with the prediabetes door).

**The sign-up funnel.** Unique visitors with `gdm_door_shown` where
`surface = landing`, against unique visitors with `gdm_onboarding_step` where
`step = told`. Between those two events she leaves this door for a sign-in page
and an email that carry the first door's name, and a magic link that opens in
the device's default browser, which is not the in-app browser most first
visitors arrive in. A poor ratio here means "check that path first", not
"nobody wants it". Read it beside KL-T1, never instead of it.
```

---

## 3. What Tier 1 does not build, and why (PRD §6.2)

- **No router that detects number-shaped questions.** "Tier 1 has no engine box for it to intercept … and the research's own saved regex nets caught 2 of 13 and 4 of 23 on this very corpus." It arrives with the read box (§5.2).
- **No comparison of a meal against the plan.** "That is F-PLANREAD, Tier 2."
- **No product-authored food content and no label** in front of a pregnant user. Everything she reads is her own words or a line from the bank.
- **No redirect of a GDM profile away from `/check`.** The door links to no prediabetes route (Task 1.2's test), signs in straight to `/gdm/start`, and has no tab bar. A person who types `/check` by hand is using the public prediabetes door as anyone can — where the `pregnancy` route "remains the backstop on every other path" (PRD §7.1). A server-side redirect would mean editing the prediabetes shell, which is on the never-edited list: §8 item 9.

---

## 4. Release prep for `organiser` (owner's steps; no code)

1. Migration `0020` applied to production; `npm run db:governance:check` green.
2. Batch G1 `Approved` (needs `gdm-organiser` in `claims-boundary.md` — the safety owner's edit).
3. Counsel has read `gdm-privacy-notice` and the `data-flow.md` rows.
4. `GDM_DOOR_ENABLED=1`, then `NEXT_PUBLIC_GDM_DOOR=landing,organiser` (never `1`). The build refuses anything else (Task 0.2).
5. Rehearse the revert before the first production flip (§7.3 step 6, G-09): `npm run config:production-check` proves the revert build loads closed; a preview with both variables unset proves export and erase still answer.
6. Fill in the two owner rows added at the review's premise gate (§1): who reviews batch G1 and by when, and where the first fifty door users come from (G-33).

---

## 5. Tier 2 — gated modules (not counted in the main sequence)

Each module starts to **release** only when its gate is open. The gate comes first; the work follows. Code may be built and merged dark ahead of its gate — the production guard (Task 0.2) is what stops it shipping, not the merge. Build order (PRD §9 Step 3): F-IDEAS-GDM → F-ROUTER and the read box → F-PLANREAD. All of Tier 2 puts product-authored food content in front of a pregnant user; none of it is reachable until the guard sees S1–S3 `Signed` (and S4 for the read).

**KL-T1 pauses this whole section** if it fires (PRD §9.2): check `launch-controls.md` §14.1 before starting a Tier 2 module.

> **Review amendments to all of §5 (read before starting any module).**
> - **G-02: "has not fired" is not enough. No module here starts until KL-T1 has been *read*,** which needs fifty door users. Engineering hours are cheap and so are the evals. What is not cheap is the S3 reviewer's entry-by-entry pass on a bank nobody has yet shown demand for, and the carrying cost of a fourth deep module, a second prompt and a metered route on a solo codebase. If the line is still unread at eight weeks, that is a distribution finding for the owner, not a green light.
> - **G-29 (Task G3.1): a vague description must never read as "about".** "About" means the model's estimate range overlaps her figure, and a description with no portions honestly yields a range wide enough to overlap almost anything. So the vaguer she is, the likelier she is reassured. Make the escape arithmetic, in `compareToPlan`: when the estimate is wider than the band it is compared with (for a single figure, wider than twice `ABOUT_TOLERANCE` of it; for a range she typed, wider than that range), return `unclear` with `missing: "portion"`. Add the case beside the symmetry test, and one per unit to the fixed panel. Like `ABOUT_TOLERANCE` it is **[A]** and belongs in S1's Verdict Semantics entry.
> - **G-50 (Tasks G3.2, G3.3): pin the privacy of the second model client.** `privacy-minimal.test.ts` asserts `store: false` on `lib/pal/openai-client.ts` and nowhere else, and `plan-read-model.ts` builds its own request. Add two source pins: `/store:\s*false/` in `lib/pal/gdm/plan-read-model.ts`, and no `console.` call in `app/api/gdm/read/route.ts`.
> - **G-16:** `/api/gdm/read` uses `gdmRouteGuard()` (Task 2.1).
> - **G-66:** the Tier 2 banks enter `EXTRA_SOURCES`, so every string in them is read by the repo's nineteen claim families as well as this plan's nine. The 26 strings drafted in this section were scanned on 2026-09-20 and are clean. The S3 reviewer's idea entries will be new text: run `npx vitest run tests/unit/pal/claims-boundary-copy.test.ts` before Task G1.1 is called done, not after.
> - **Correction to §8 item 16:** renaming the `gdm_*` events would not hide this door from the analytics vendor. Umami records the page **path**, and every page here lives under `/gdm/`.

### 5.1 F-IDEAS-GDM · meal ideas by occasion — **Gates: S1, S2, S3, and the `gdm-food-ideas` class (S5)**

**What opens it.** S1–S3 read `Signed` in `docs/safety/gdm-gates.md`; the safety owner has added `gdm-food-ideas` to `claims-boundary.md`; **every bank entry carries the S3 reviewer's sign-off in its ledger row** (PRD acceptance: reviewed entry by entry) and the rows are `Approved`. Then `NEXT_PUBLIC_GDM_DOOR=landing,organiser,ideas`.

**What it is.** A bounded, reviewed bank in the `lib/pal/guide-ideas.ts` pattern — fixed entries, deterministic rotation, no free generation — grouped by the six occasions, available from the first session because it needs no figure. Positive-only. One tap saves an idea into My meals. A vegetarian filter (story 3); cuisines are served by the bank's breadth, which the reviewer controls, not by a second filter (§8 item 14). **Must never say** that an idea suits her, produces a reading, or is her plan; any quantity; any food to limit; **no sentence pairs a food with a fasting number** — the bedtime snack is an occasion like any other (row 11).

**The build.**

- [ ] **Task G1.1 — the bank and its deterministic gate.**
  **Files:** create `lib/pal/gdm/ideas.ts`, `tests/unit/pal/gdm-ideas.test.ts`; modify `tests/unit/pal/gdm-copy.test.ts` — append the bank to `GDM_BANKS`. The idea bank is not a record of named strings, so it does **not** go through `toRows`; spell the adapter out, one ledger row per occasion, Copy ID with hyphens: `{ name: "lib/pal/gdm/ideas.ts", rows: Object.fromEntries(GDM_OCCASIONS.map((occasion) => [\`gdm-ideas-${occasion.replace("_", "-")}\`, GDM_IDEA_BANK[occasion].map((idea) => idea.text)])) }` The surface's UI labels are a separate export from the same module — `GDM_IDEAS_COPY = { "gdm-ideas-controls": { title: …, save: …, vegetarianOnly: … } }`, a record of named strings like `GDM_COPY` — and that one **does** go through `toRows` as a second `GDM_BANKS` entry; `tests/unit/pal/claims-boundary-copy.test.ts` (`EXTRA_SOURCES` += `lib/pal/gdm/ideas.ts`), `lib/gdm-door-flag.ts` (`ideas` rows), the ledger (rows `gdm-ideas-breakfast`, `-snack-morning`, `-lunch`, `-snack-afternoon`, `-dinner`, `-snack-bedtime`, `gdm-ideas-controls`; class `gdm-food-ideas`; `Pending`).
  **Interfaces:** `type GdmIdea = { id: string; occasion: GdmOccasion; text: string; vegetarian: boolean }`; `GDM_IDEA_BANK: Record<GdmOccasion, ReadonlyArray<{ text: string; vegetarian: boolean }>>`; `GDM_IDEAS: readonly GdmIdea[]` (ids `` `${occasion}-${n}` ``); `gdmIdeasFor(occasion, rotation, options?: { count?: number; vegetarianOnly?: boolean }): GdmIdea[]` — the same `(rotation × count) % bank.length` wrap as `ideasFrom` in `lib/pal/guide-ideas.ts`, re-implemented here (the prediabetes bank is not imported).
  **Test to write first** (`gdm-ideas.test.ts`), one assertion each: at least six ideas per occasion and at least three vegetarian; ids unique; every idea `classifyClinicalRisk(text) === null`; no digit; at most 59 characters; positive-only (`/\b(?:avoid|limit|skip|cut|without|instead of|no )\b/i` absent — the `guide-ideas.test.ts` regex); no reading word (`/fasting|overnight|reading|number|spike|sugar/i` absent — row 11); none of `PREGNANCY_CAUTION_TOKENS`, exported from the bank module: `/\b(?:raw|rare|runny|soft[- ]boiled|sushi|sashimi|smoked|deli|salami|prosciutto|p[âa]t[ée]|liver|brie|camembert|feta|blue cheese|queso fresco|unpasteuri[sz]ed|sprouts|swordfish|shark|mackerel|tilefish|tuna|alcohol|wine|beer)\b/i`; rotation deterministic and two consecutive loads disjoint; `vegetarianOnly` returns only vegetarian ideas.
  **The caution list is an engineering guard [A], not clinical validation:** any food whose pregnancy guidance is a *quantity limit* or a *preparation rule* cannot sit in a bank that may carry neither. It lowers the reviewer's load; the S3 reviewer's entry-by-entry pass is the gate and may amend the list.
  **Drafting the entries:** the executor drafts to the rules above — meal-shaped lines naming foods only, in the register of `GUIDE_IDEA_BANK` (for example "scrambled eggs with peppers and spinach", "lentil soup with a side salad", "plain yogurt with berries and walnuts", "apple slices with peanut butter") — and files every line `Pending`. No line is the executor's to approve.

Analytics test: the two events in `ALLOWED_NAMES` / `oneOfEach`.

Here there is no band and no fixed answer — the figure is hers — so the property is different: **every idea, sent to the read (story 5), gives a stable outcome inside the closed set, on a fixed panel of figures.** The table it writes is also the S3 and S4 reviewers' engineering evidence for each entry.

Each idea × each cell runs twenty times through `readAgainstPlan` with the real model client. **A cell passes** when all twenty runs return the same outcome (one word-state, or `unclear`) and none throws; mixed outcomes ⇒ `unstable`; a transport failure ⇒ re-run up to three times, then `inconclusive` and the eval fails loudly (the A-27 rule). The file is keyed on `PLAN_READ_PROMPT_VERSION` and the model id.

**Acceptance (PRD).** Every entry carries the reviewer's sign-off in the ledger; the copy tests pass over the bank; no entry contains a digit; claim class `gdm-food-ideas`.

### 5.2 F-ROUTER · catch what the read box must not answer — **Gates: ships only with the read box, under `read` (S1–S4)**

**What opens it.** Nothing on its own: the router has no surface without the read box, and the read box has no purpose without the read. It is built second because F-PLANREAD's input goes through it, and it releases with §5.3.

**What it is.** A non-generative, precedence-ordered router in the pattern of `lib/pal/clinical-risk.ts`. **The existing clinical routes fire first — all nine, `pregnancy` included, until S2 is signed.** Then number-, dose-, delivery- and device-shaped input returns one fixed ledger line and an "add to my questions" action. Everything else goes to the read.

**Recall ceiling, stated in the module's docstring and in §14.5.** The research's own nets caught 2 of 13 and 4 of 23 on this corpus. This router will miss most of what it is built to catch. So: F-PLANREAD's own must-never-say rules are the real guard, not the router; and the router's counts are a **floor**.

**The build.**

- [ ] **Task G2.1 — the router.**
  **Files:** create `lib/pal/gdm/router.ts`, `tests/unit/pal/gdm-router.test.ts`; modify `tests/unit/pal/gdm-a1c-free.test.ts` — exempt `lib/pal/gdm/router.ts` from the medication-**field** scan only, with the reason in a comment ("the router must name the words it catches; it declares no field").
  **Interfaces:** `GDM_PARK_ROUTES = ["number", "dose", "delivery", "device"] as const`; `type GdmParkRoute`; `type GdmRouterBranch = { id: string; route: GdmParkRoute; pattern: RegExp }`; `GDM_ROUTER_BRANCHES: readonly GdmRouterBranch[]` — **one branch per idea, no top-level alternation inside a pattern**, so a probe can name the branch it proves; `type GdmRouting = { kind: "clinical"; route: ClinicalRoute } | { kind: "park"; route: GdmParkRoute; branch: string } | { kind: "meal" }`; `routeGdmInput(text: string): GdmRouting` — `classifyClinicalRisk` first, then the branches in array order, word-boundary matching on the lower-cased text.
  **Test to write first:** (1) **one must-match probe per branch, not per pattern** — `expect(new Set(PROBES.map((p) => p.branch))).toEqual(new Set(GDM_ROUTER_BRANCHES.map((b) => b.id)))`, and each probe asserts `routeGdmInput(text)` equals `{ kind: "park", route, branch }` with **that** branch id, so a branch shadowed by an earlier one fails (the research's "fourth `\b` variant" lesson); (2) one must-not-match probe per route — ordinary meals that look number-shaped route to `meal` ("two eggs and toast", "a six inch sub", "half a cup of rice and dal", "baked potato at dinner"); (3) precedence — one probe for each of the nine `CLINICAL_ROUTES` returns `{ kind: "clinical", route }`, **`pregnancy` among them** ("until S2" in the test title); (4) a message that is both ("why was my fasting 106, should I take more insulin") routes `clinical`; (5) every `GDM_IDEAS` text routes to `meal`.
  **Starting branches** (the executor adds probes as it adds branches; none ships without one): number — `fasting`, `readings?`, `my numbers?`, `spik(?:e|ed|es|ing)`, `mmol`, `post[- ]?prandial`, `(?:one|two|1|2)[- ]hour`; dose — `glyburide`, `night ?time shot`, `put on (?:meds|medication|tablets)`; delivery — `induc`, `c-?section`, `ca?esarean`, `due date`, `\d{2} weeks`; device — `meter`, `test strips?`, `lancets?`, `sensor`.
  **Run:** `npm run test:pal -- gdm-router gdm-a1c-free gdm-pregnancy-route` → FAIL (module not found), then PASS.

- [ ] **Task G2.2 — the fixed line and the park action.**
  **Files:** create `lib/pal/gdm/read-copy.ts` (`GDM_READ_COPY`, the Tier 2 result bank — this task files `gdm-router-park`; Task G3.3 files the rest), `components/gdm/park-action.tsx`; modify `tests/unit/pal/gdm-copy.test.ts` (`GDM_BANKS` += `GDM_READ_COPY`), `EXTRA_SOURCES`, the flag (`read` rows), the ledger.
  **Row:** `gdm-router-park` — `line`: That one is for your care team. · `park`: Add to my questions
  The row's class is `gdm-plan-read` in the draft; the safety owner may prefer `gdm-organiser` (it names the care team and says nothing about food) — §8 item 8.
  **Test to write first:** in `gdm-router.test.ts` — the line says nothing about the content of the question (`/common|normal|can happen|worth|usually/i` absent) and is one sentence; `park-action.tsx` POSTs `{ kind: "ask", body: { text: <her own typed text, verbatim>, note: null, asked: false, answer: null } }` and tracks `gdm_ask_parked` with `from: "read_box"` — a count only (Task G2.3 adds `"read_box"` to that event's closed `from` enum; the Task 2.2 no-free-text scan covers the call).

  **§14.5 text:** "KL-T2 — four weeks after `read` is live, at 50 door users or more. Numerator: count of `gdm_routed` (every `side`). Denominator: count of `gdm_read_run` where `state` is `heavier`, `about` or `lighter`. **Fires when Numerator ÷ Denominator > 2.** Fires ⇒ stop adding food features and re-read. **The numerator is a floor**: the router's recall on this corpus was 2 of 13 and 4 of 23, so the bias runs against this line firing — when it fires it is credible; when it does not it proves little. Read beside it: the split of `gdm_read_run` by `state` — whether `lighter` is as common as the under-eating hazard suggests. Watched, not targeted."
  **Run:** `npm run test:pal -- gdm-kill-lines`, `npx vitest run tests/unit/client/analytics.test.ts` → FAIL then PASS.

### 5.3 F-PLANREAD · a meal read against her own figure — **Gates: S1, S2, S3, S4, the `gdm-plan-read` class, and the new engine path**

**What opens it.** All four gates `Signed`; `gdm-plan-read` in `claims-boundary.md` with S1's Verdict Semantics entry for the three word-states; every `read` row `Approved` — **`gdm-read-lighter` is its own row so that S4's ruling is one row's status**; Tasks G3.1–G3.5 green with the panel eval logged. **The engine path may be built and tested on the fixed panel before S4. It is never reachable by a user before S4** — Task G3.3's tests pin that, and the production guard refuses `read` until S4 reads `Signed`.

**What it is.** Input: a meal description, the occasion, and the figure and unit F-PLANKEEP holds for that occasion. Output: **one of three word-states — heavier than your plan says · about what your plan says · lighter than your plan says —** one reason in words, and one food-level line in whichever direction applies. **These are not the three labels: no reuse of `lib/pal/labels.ts`**, which is defined against meal-composition rules and an A1C band (Task 0.5 already fails the build if a GDM file imports it). Units: **grams per occasion, or choices where her sheet states what one choice is. Anything else — servings, none given, no figure, two entries that differ, a figure that cannot be read as written — means no read, and the surface says why.** No digit appears in any result string except her own figure shown back with its date.

**Design that keeps the must-never-say list structural, not hopeful:**
- **The model never sees her figure.** It estimates the described meal and returns only `{ kind: "estimate", lowGrams, highGrams }` or `{ kind: "unclear", missing: "portion" | "item" }`. It therefore cannot judge, alter or echo her figure.
- **The comparison is deterministic code**, in her unit: grams as they are; choices as the estimate divided by what *her sheet* says one choice is. The quotient is never displayed — this is the arithmetic PRD §6.3 names ("the comparison underneath is arithmetic against a clinician's figure"), not a conversion on screen.
- **Every string she reads comes from the bank** (`GDM_READ_COPY`), chosen by the word-state. The model authors no user-visible text, so "no digit in any result string" and "no estimate shown" hold by construction.
- **A range she typed is the band.** "30–45 g" ⇒ *about* means the estimate overlaps 30–45. A single figure gets `ABOUT_TOLERANCE = 0.15` either side **[A]** — a calibration knob that S1's Verdict Semantics entry must set (§8 item 5).

**The build.**

- [ ] **Task G3.1 — the deterministic core (deep module 4).**
  **Files:** create `lib/pal/gdm/plan-read.ts`, `tests/unit/pal/gdm-plan-read.test.ts`.
  **Interfaces:** `PLAN_READ_STATES = ["heavier", "about", "lighter"] as const`; `type PlanFigure = { low: number; high: number }`; `parseFigure(text: string): PlanFigure | null` (accepts `45`, `45 g`, `45g`, `30-45`, `30–45 g`, `30 to 45`, `3`, `2 to 3 choices`; anything else ⇒ `null`); `parseChoiceMeans(text: string | null): number | null` (`15 g`, `15 grams`, `15`); `type NoReadWhy = "no_figure" | "plans_differ" | "unit_unsupported" | "figure_unreadable"`; `heldFigure(plans: StoredPlan[], occasion: GdmOccasion): { ok: true; figure: PlanFigure; unit: "grams" | "choices"; choiceGrams: number | null; shown: string; enteredOn: string } | { ok: false; why: NoReadWhy }`; `compareToPlan(estimate: { lowGrams: number; highGrams: number }, held): PlanReadState`; `ABOUT_TOLERANCE`.
  **Test to write first:** no current plan, or no figure for the occasion ⇒ `no_figure`; two current plans that differ for the occasion ⇒ `plans_differ` (never pick one); `servings` / `none` ⇒ `unit_unsupported`; `choices` with no readable `choiceMeans` ⇒ `unit_unsupported`; "a fist of rice" ⇒ `figure_unreadable`; a replaced plan is ignored; `shown` is her string byte-for-byte; range and single-figure comparisons in both directions, plus the three boundary cases on each side; **`lighter` is reachable and symmetric with `heavier`** (mirror-image inputs give mirror-image states — the app never rewards under-eating by making `lighter` harder to reach); source pin — the module imports nothing from `../labels`, `../service`, `../a1c`.
  **Run:** `npm run test:pal -- gdm-plan-read` → FAIL (module not found), then PASS.

- [ ] **Task G3.2 — the engine path: prompt, model client, orchestration.**
  **Files:** create `lib/pal/gdm/plan-read-prompt.ts` (`PLAN_READ_PROMPT_VERSION`, `buildPlanReadPrompt({ description, occasion })`), `lib/pal/gdm/plan-read-model.ts` (`interface PlanReadModel { estimate(input): Promise<PlanReadModelOutput> }`, `PlanReadModelOutputSchema` (zod, strict), `createOpenAIPlanReadModel(options?)`), extend `lib/pal/gdm/plan-read.ts` with `readAgainstPlan`; extend `lib/pal/gdm/read-copy.ts`; **one export-only touch** on `lib/pal/openai-client.ts`: add `export` to `createTransport`, `createWithConnectionRetry` and the `OpenAIResponsesTransport` type so the new client reuses the transport, the 10-second timeout and the single-paid-attempt retry rule. No behaviour changes; `tests/unit/pal/engine-regression.test.ts` stays green. (§8 item 12: if the owner wants zero touches on an engine file, copy the sixty lines instead — D3's split would need them anyway.)
  **Interfaces:** `type PlanReadResult = { kind: "read"; state: PlanReadState; lines: { lead: string; why: string; next: string | null }; figureShown: string; figureDate: string } | { kind: "unclear"; ask: string } | { kind: "no_read"; why: NoReadWhy; line: string } | { kind: "routed"; routing: Exclude<GdmRouting, { kind: "meal" }> }`; `readAgainstPlan(input: { description: string; occasion: GdmOccasion; plans: StoredPlan[] }, deps: { model: PlanReadModel }): Promise<PlanReadResult>` — order: `routeGdmInput` → `heldFigure` (**no figure ⇒ return before any model call**) → `model.estimate` → `compareToPlan` → strings from `GDM_READ_COPY`. The request uses `store: false` and a strict JSON schema, as `createOpenAIPalModelClient` does. **The prompt is given the description and the occasion — never her figure, her unit or her plan.**
  **Rows** (class `gdm-plan-read`, `Pending`; drafts for the S3 reviewer and, for the fourth, S4):
  `gdm-read-controls` — `title`: Read a meal against my plan · `occasion`: Which occasion · `describe`: Describe the meal · `go`: Read it · `against`: Compared with {figure}, entered on {date}
  `gdm-read-heavier` — `lead`: Heavier than your plan says. · `why`: This description reads as more carbohydrate than the figure you entered for this occasion. · `next`: One option: a smaller portion of the starchy part, keeping the protein and vegetables as they are.
  `gdm-read-about` — `lead`: About what your plan says. · `why`: This description reads as close to the figure you entered for this occasion.
  `gdm-read-lighter` — `lead`: Lighter than your plan says. · `why`: This description reads as less carbohydrate than the figure you entered for this occasion. · `next`: One option: add a carbohydrate food your plan already lists for this occasion. **← S4 rules on this row.**
  `gdm-read-unclear` — `portion`: How much of each food was it? A rough size is enough, like a small bowl or a couple of slices. · `item`: Which foods were in it?
  `gdm-read-none` — `no_figure`: There is no figure in your plan for this occasion yet, so there is nothing to read this against. · `unit_unsupported`: Your plan counts in a way this read does not cover, so there is no read. · `plans_differ`: Two entries in your plan differ for this occasion, so there is no read until you know which one stands. · `figure_unreadable`: The figure in your plan could not be read as written, so there is no read.
  In `gdm-copy.test.ts` the `reduction` family gains `exemptRows: ["gdm-read-heavier"]` — the one row that must name a smaller portion, because PRD §6.3 requires an adjustment line in **both** directions. No other exemption.
  **Test to write first** (extend `gdm-plan-read.test.ts`, stub model): no figure ⇒ `no_read` and **a throwing model is never called**; a routed input ⇒ `routed` and the model is never called; `unclear` ⇒ the bank's question for that `missing` value; `heavier` and `lighter` both carry a non-null `next`, `about` carries `null`; **no digit in any returned string except `figureShown` and `figureDate`** (`JSON.stringify` of the result with those two fields removed matches no `/\d/`); `figureShown` is her string verbatim; the model stub records its input and it contains neither the figure nor the unit; the same input twice gives the same result.
  **Run:** `npm run test:pal -- gdm-plan-read gdm-copy gdm-pregnancy-route engine-regression`, `npm run typecheck` → FAIL then PASS.

- [ ] **Task G3.3 — the API, the read box, and the proof it is unreachable.**
  **Files:** create `app/api/gdm/read/route.ts` (`createGdmReadHandlers({ db?, getSession?, model? })`), `app/gdm/(door)/read/page.tsx`, `components/gdm/read-box.tsx`, `tests/unit/pal/gdm-read-route.test.ts`; modify `proxy.ts` (matcher += `"/api/gdm/read"`) and `lib/pal/rate-limit.ts` (`matchRouteLimit`: `/api/gdm/read` ⇒ `{ kind: "abuse", bucket: "gdm_read_ip", failClosed: true }`, plus the `LimitBucket` / `BUCKETS` entry at the `/api/check` budget) with `tests/unit/pal/rate-limit.test.ts` and `tests/unit/proxy.test.ts`; `components/gdm/nav.tsx`.

## 6. Not planned — recorded, with the PRD's reason

| Feature | Status in the PRD | Why no task exists |
|---|---|---|
| **F-READ-GDM** · a qualitative read with no figure | **Closed by D1(a)** | "The owner chose no read without her own figure. Its too-light handling survives inside F-PLANREAD" |
| **F-IMPORT** · handout importer with unit conversion | **Not needed** | "Under D1(a) she types her own figure and the read stays in her unit. A photo of the sheet is already kept by F-PLANKEEP" |
| **F-STARTERS** · question starters | Specified, **not opened** | "Near the clinical line, as v1.1's F-DOCTOR. D4(b) did not cover it. Needs its own class" |
| **F-LEARN-GDM** · a general explainer | Specified, **not opened** | "No class fits; same shape as v1.1's F-NUMBERS. D4(b) did not cover it" |
| **F-JOURNAL** · food beside readings | Tier 3 | "This is v1.1's F-DIARY. It changes the intended-use statement and the regulatory posture. D4(b) opened ideas and reads, not this. Recorded, not recommended" |
| **F-EXPERIENCES** · what happened to others | Not built | "The largest single want, and the one the subreddit already serves at 21 of 25 answered. Moderation plus medical claims in pregnancy is the highest-risk surface available." Whether the door may simply say that communities exist is left to the safety owner |
| **F-REMIND** · testing reminders | Not built | "0 of 12 monitoring posts are in the audience, and a testing schedule is clinician-set. Not built; revisit only if users ask for it" |

Also out of scope by PRD §4.2: a glucose tracker, device or meter advice, postpartum, any hand-off into the prediabetes door, a second codebase.

## 7. Test and rollout plan

### 7.1 Gates on every PR

```bash
npm run lint && npm run typecheck && npm run test:pal && npm run contract && npm test
npm run e2e -- tests/smoke/gdm-door.spec.ts
PAL_E2E_GDM_DOOR=1 npm run e2e -- tests/smoke/gdm-door.spec.ts
```

The flag-off leg proves the prediabetes door is byte-for-byte unchanged and every `/gdm/*` route is a 404; the flag-on leg proves the door. Both must be green, and CI runs both (Task 0.3).

**The F-CALM-GDM audit checklist** (the last task of every phase; PRD §6.2 F-CALM-GDM, `DESIGN.md` §1):

- [ ] `npm run test:pal -- gdm-copy` green over the phase's new rows — the two banned strings, grading words, blame grammar, gamification, medication words, delivery words, digits, reduction words, ledger parity, no literal copy.
- [ ] No streak, score, badge, progress bar or tick-state anywhere on the new surface. No countdown framed as pressure.
- [ ] Nothing makes eating less read as success (G4). Nothing on screen grades a person or a day.
- [ ] No sentence about a reading, a dose, a delivery decision, the pregnancy or the baby — including in empty states, errors and `aria-label`s.
- [ ] Every figure on screen was typed by her. Empty renders as empty.
- [ ] Rails: one accent; 44px targets; icons only from `components/icons.tsx`; no dark band; health information never in `--text-soft`; visible focus; axe clean at 375px and 1280px.
- [ ] The working name leads; "Prediabetes Pal" is not a headline on the surface (D7).

### 7.3 Rollout order and the flag

1. **Merge PR-0 … PR-6 with both variables unset everywhere.** No visible change; every `/gdm/*` route 404s. Previews may set `NEXT_PUBLIC_GDM_DOOR=1` and `GDM_DOOR_ENABLED=1` for review.
2. **Put §8.1's three class rows and S1–S4 to the safety owner today**, in parallel with the build (PRD §9 Step 1). That is the owner's action; the executor's part is the batch in §7.5.
3. **`gdm-organiser` exists and `landing`'s rows are `Approved`** ⇒ `GDM_DOOR_ENABLED=1`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_GDM_DOOR=landing`. The landing is live; `/gdm/start` holds.
4. **Batch G1 `Approved`, migration `0020` applied, counsel has read the privacy notice** ⇒ `NEXT_PUBLIC_GDM_DOOR=landing,organiser`. The four-week clock for KL-T1, KL-D1 and D7 starts (`launch-controls.md` §14). PRD §11.1: keep the first group small — post the link in one place, not everywhere.
5. **Post-deploy verification**, first five minutes, after any deploy that changes the value: `GET /api/health` → `gdmDoor` shows exactly the expected surfaces and `flagTwins.gdmDoor = "on"` · `/gdm` renders at 375px · sign in from `/gdm` lands on `/gdm/start`, not `/welcome` · park one question and delete it · `/check` and `/home` are unchanged · `gdm_door_shown` arrives in Umami within the hour (if not, assume analytics is blind before assuming nobody came).
6. **Revert (a fired kill line, or any incident) (G-09):** unset `GDM_DOOR_ENABLED` and redeploy. That is the whole lever. The guard treats a missing twin as a closed door, so the redeploy builds, the client flag is inlined empty, and every GDM surface is dark, the landing included. Unsetting `NEXT_PUBLIC_GDM_DOOR` instead works equally well; do the other one afterwards, at leisure. **No data migration, and nothing is deleted:** export and erase keep working with the door closed (Task 1.3). **Where this is rehearsed:** `npm run config:production-check` (Task 0.2), which runs the production guard. *Not* on a preview: there `VERCEL_ENV` is `preview`, the guard never runs, and a rehearsal would pass whether or not the revert works. What a preview can confirm, and should before step 4, is that export and erase still return 200 with the door closed. As first written this step unset only the twin under a guard that refused that state, so the redeploy would have failed and the open door kept serving.
7. **Tier 2:** `ideas` joins the list when S1–S3 are `Signed` and its rows `Approved`; `read` when S4 is too and the panel eval is logged. The build refuses either until then.
8. **Retire the flag:** once KL-T1 has been read and survived, one PR deletes the flag-off branches and the dark smoke describe. `GDM_DOOR_ENABLED` stays — it is the kill switch, not a rollout device.

### 7.4 What is measured, and where to read it

All of it lives in `docs/ops/launch-controls.md` §14 (Task 6.3; §14.5 from Task G2.3): KL-T1, D7's trigger, KL-D1, KL-T2, and PRD §9.1's directional reads, each with its events, its numerator, its denominator, the 50-door-user floor and — where the plaintext columns allow it — an exact database cross-check. Behavioural only; gates and directions, not targets. **Nothing measures glucose, weight, medication, delivery or any pregnancy outcome, and nothing will.** The tone audit's outcome (PRD §9.1: feedback mentions of "scary", "judged", "guilt", "confusing") has no instrument in Tier 1 — the door has no feedback box, and adding one is a free-text surface for health data. It is read from whatever reaches the support inbox (§8 item 16).

## 8. Open questions for the owner

Where the PRD is ambiguous this plan did not invent an answer; each item states the default the plan uses so execution is not blocked. **The first two are the ones PRD v1.1 itself asks the owner to confirm.**

1. **D7's re-anchored trigger — confirm or replace.** PRD §10.1: "The ruling's trigger was cut off and named the skipped test; **re-anchored to surviving the four-week Tier 1 line [A]**. The owner should confirm or replace that trigger." *Plan default:* as re-anchored, and made precise in `launch-controls.md` §14.2 — KL-T1 read at 50 door users or more **and not fired**. One consequence to rule on: a line left **unread** because fewer than 50 door users arrived in four weeks is not survival, so spending stays closed until it is read. Alternatives: a date ("four weeks live, whatever the count"), or a lower floor for D7 alone.
2. **The scope-versus-clinical-line framing — confirm it.** PRD v1.1's header reads the two rulings of 2026-09-19 this way: D4(b) "moves the **scope boundary** — who the product is for — and supersedes the first direction to that extent. **The clinical line does not move**: no product sentence about a reading, a dose, a delivery decision, the pregnancy or the baby." This plan is built on that reading: Tier 1 carries no product-authored food content at all; Tier 2 carries food content only, behind S1–S5. If the owner instead meant the first direction ("every pain gets a general, non-clinical answer; no feature may touch the medical boundary") to keep binding after D4(b), then §5 is closed, F-MYMEALS is the only food surface, and the landing line never gains "meal ideas". Nothing in Tier 1 changes either way.
3. **KL-D1's clock.** PRD §9.2 says "after four weeks" without saying of what. *Plan default:* four weeks after `organiser` is live — F-PLANKEEP takes figures from day one, and reading the line then tells the owner whether F-PLANREAD is worth building **before** it is built (PRD §11: "the most expensive feature here and serves the smaller half of the audience"). Alternative: four weeks after the read box is live, which reads it only after the money is spent.
4. **One `organiser` surface, or F-ASKLIST first?** PRD §9 asks for "one ledger batch for all Tier 1 strings"; §11.1 calls F-ASKLIST "the cheapest live signal". *Plan default:* one surface, everything opens together. Splitting it is a ten-line change when the owner wants the list live alone.
5. **What "about" means.** A figure she typed as a range is its own band. For a single figure the plan uses ±15% **[A]** (`ABOUT_TOLERANCE`). This is the content of S1's Verdict Semantics entry — the safety owner sets it; the constant is a knob, not a finding.
6. **If S4 cannot clear.** PRD §11.1: "the read ships with the 'heavier / about' states only and D2 returns to the owner." That leaves a meal that reads lighter with nothing true to show — calling it "about" would be false, and hiding it rewards the under-eating the door is built not to reward. *Plan default:* build all three states; if S4 fails, stop and bring D2 back before shipping any read. Not planned: a two-state variant.
7. **The landing line.** PRD §3's working line opens "Diagnosed with gestational diabetes…", which the existing `diagnose` family in `claims-boundary-copy.test.ts` refuses on any page — and PRD §0 says those families do not change. *Plan default:* "Told you have gestational diabetes and waiting for your dietitian? …", matching the onboarding question. Alternative: the safety owner grants `app/gdm/page.tsx` an `exemptSources` entry with a reason — a change to the audit, which this plan will not make on its own.
8. **Clinical cards on the list, and inside the read box.** On My questions only the four routes PRD §6.2 names raise a card. The `pregnancy` row's copy is about A1C ranges and would fire on nearly every question this audience types; nothing is read behind the list, so not rendering it there is not S2 — *the safety owner should confirm that reading.* Inside the Tier 2 read box every clinical route still **blocks the read**; the first four show their own approved rows, and the five below them show the router's fixed line (`gdm-router-park`) rather than prediabetes-framed copy. Also theirs: whether `gdm-router-park` files under `gdm-plan-read` or `gdm-organiser`.
9. **"`/check` is not reachable from the GDM profile" (PRD §7.1).** *Plan default:* the door links to no prediabetes route, signs in straight to `/gdm/start`, and has no tab bar; a person who types `/check` by hand meets the public door, where the `pregnancy` route is the backstop. A server-side redirect for GDM profiles needs one line in the prediabetes shell — a never-edited file. The owner may grant it.
10. **Shared sign-in.** `/signin`, the check-email page and the magic-link email all say "Prediabetes Pal". D7 says spend nothing. *Plan default:* reuse them; the working name leads everywhere the door itself renders. Alternative: a `/gdm/signin` page and a second email template.
11. **The privacy notice.** *Plan default:* a door-scoped notice at `/gdm/privacy`, so nothing under `app/(app)/` is edited. Counsel may prefer one notice at `/privacy`; that page's phrases are pinned by `privacy-stateful.test.ts` in lockstep with three other documents, so it is a counsel-led change, not a task here.
12. **The one touch on an engine file.** Task G3.2 adds `export` to three names in `lib/pal/openai-client.ts` and changes no behaviour. *Plan default:* take it. Alternative: copy about sixty lines and touch nothing.
13. **Where the sheet photo lives.** *Plan default:* encrypted in Postgres (at most three, downscaled on the device), so erase, export and the account cascade cover it with no second deletion path. Alternatives: private Blob storage (needs its own deletion path — the class of bug `lib/server/blob.ts` was written to end) or on-device only (lost with the phone; "in my pocket" only on one device).
14. **Cuisines (F-IDEAS-GDM story 3).** *Plan default:* a vegetarian filter, and breadth the S3 reviewer controls. A cuisine filter is a second taxonomy to review; add it if users ask.
15. **The read's cost.** PRD §0: pricing does not change. *Plan default:* the read is free, metered per IP at the `/api/check` budget, signed-in only. A per-user daily cap needs a counter the door does not have; add it if reads ever appear as a cost line.
16. **Event names, and the tone read.** `gdm_*` event names tell the analytics vendor that an anonymous, cookieless visitor used a gestational-diabetes door. PRD §7.4 allows these events and forbids joining the flag to an identity; nothing does. If that is still too much, rename them neutrally (`door2_*`) in Task 0.6 — before launch, not after. Separately, PRD §9.1's tone measure has no instrument (§7.4); the owner reads the support inbox.
17. **An evidence-pack row for ACOG.** `gdm-wait-structure` cites `FDA-GENERAL-WELLNESS, FTC-HEALTH-COMPLIANCE` like the other organiser rows. If the safety owner wants the quote tied to its own evidence row, that row is added to `docs/safety/evidence-pack.md` with their review — before the row can be `Approved`.
18. **Landing indexing.** *Plan default:* `noindex`, out of the sitemap — a working name and no spend (D7). Flip both when D7's trigger is met.
