# /autoplan review record

Reviewed 2026-09-20 on `main` at `7ad39eb` (this plan is untracked). Restore point: `~/.gstack/projects/revora/main-autoplan-restore-20260920-002648.md` (verified byte-identical to the plan as it stood).

**Decision IDs use the prefix `G-`**, not `A-`. The guide-redesign review's `A-`/`R-` series is already cited from source (`next.config.ts`, `TODOS.md`, test names), so reusing it here would make "A-94" ambiguous.

**How to read this record.** Each finding carries a decision and the amendment it implies. **Status: APPROVED by the owner at the final gate on 2026-09-20, option A (tiered amendments), and applied.** The eight critical and high findings (G-09, G-10, G-12, G-27, G-30, G-32, G-44, G-52) are rewritten into the task steps they change and marked `(G-nn)`. Every other finding sits in a "Review amendments" note under the heading of the task it changes. The record below is the reasoning and the evidence; it was written before the tasks were touched, so where it says "as written" it means the plan as the author left it, which the restore point preserves.

**Pipeline degradations, stated once.** (1) The gstack helpers are installed under `~/.claude/skills/igstack/`, not `~/.claude/skills/gstack/`, so the autoplan preamble's helper calls were no-ops; the restore point, slug (`revora`) and review log were done by hand. (2) **Codex is unavailable for every phase**: `codex-cli 0.144.1` hung for 300 s on a one-word prompt, then at low effort returned "You've hit your usage limit … try again at Oct 14th, 2026". Every voices table below is therefore `[subagent-only]`, its Codex column reads N/A, and no dimension can be marked CONFIRMED. The outside voice is a fresh Claude subagent with no sight of this review: independent context, same model family, so treat agreement as weaker than a true cross-model signal. One consequence: a *User Challenge* needs two models to agree against the owner's stated direction, so none can be raised in this run. Where the primary review and the subagent both disagree with an owner decision, it is surfaced as a taste decision and labelled as such. (3) No `design` binary, so no mockups were generated.

**CORRECTION, added after the final gate (read this before trusting any "single-reviewer" or "had not reported" statement below).** The outside voices were not stalled. All four subagents finished between 04:39Z and 05:08Z, within fifteen minutes of launch and **before the premise gate was asked**. Their reports sat in this session's inbox because the primary reviewer never ended its turn, and teammate messages are delivered only between turns. So both gates were put to the owner on one reviewer's analysis while three independent reports were already written. They are folded in under **"Post-gate addendum"** near the end of this record. Every statement below that the voices "had not reported" or that an agent "returned nothing" describes what the reviewer could see, not what had happened, and the sentence above that no User Challenge can be raised in this run **is withdrawn**: the CEO voice raised one (G-72).

## Phase 1 — CEO review (mode: SELECTIVE EXPANSION, auto-decided)

### Pre-review system audit

- **Tree.** `main` at `7ad39eb`, no stash. Six files are modified and uncommitted. Two matter to this plan: `DESIGN.md` (on the never-edited list, so the executor must not "fix" that diff) and `docs/safety/copy-ledger.md` (Task 0.4's parity test and every "append after the last row, today line 158" instruction read it).
- **Hot files this plan also touches** (commits in 30 days): `docs/safety/copy-ledger.md` 22, `app/globals.css` 18, `tests/unit/pal/flag-server-twins.test.ts` 9. Seven PRs that each append ledger rows will conflict with any parallel guide-redesign batch. Line numbers quoted in the plan are a snapshot, not an address.
- **Retrospective.** The repo's last two review-driven fixes were both in a production door guard (`d8fda4d` intake requires source and home; the A-94/A-100/A-101 guide-door guard). This plan adds a third guard with a new dimension (a server twin inside a surface list). Guard semantics got the most scrutiny here for that reason, and that is where the one critical finding sits (G-09).
- **Taste calibration.** Worth copying: `lib/guide-door-guard.ts` (pure, every error names its fix); the `createXHandlers(deps)` route pattern with PGlite; the account export's schema-derived denominator, which fails when a new user-scoped table is unaccounted for. Worth not repeating: source-scan regexes standing in for behaviour tests (the plan adds about fifteen); one 4,000-line global stylesheet.
- **Design doc.** No GDM-specific `/office-hours` doc exists. PRD v1.1 and `docs/handoff/2026-09-12-gestational-top5-pains-outcomes.md` are richer than one would be, so the prerequisite offer was skipped.

### Who already serves her (search check)

- **Layer 1, tried and true.** GDM apps are glucose-and-meal loggers that export a report for the clinician. They are free. Gestational Diabetes Tracker (built by a mother who had GDM, no subscription) and Malama (meter sync, AI meal analysis, nutritionists attached) own that job. PRD §11 already says "do not compete on logging".
- **Layer 2, what search says now.** The category has filled with photo-to-carb tools (SNAQ, NutriScan, Ninta). And the waiting-weeks question, "what do I eat until I see the dietitian", is answered for free by hospital and publisher pages in exactly the register Tier 1 is forbidden to use ("three small to moderate meals and one or more snacks", "pair a carbohydrate with protein").
- **Layer 3, first principles.** Every incumbent is built around the glucose log. Nobody is built around **the appointment**: the question list, the plan in the clinician's words, the one page she carries in. That job needs no food claim and no reading, so it is the one position the safety boundary does not tax. It is also the only surface here a second person sees: the printed summary lands in front of a dietitian who meets dozens of GDM patients a month. **The page is the distribution channel the plan does not mention.** Logged as a differentiation note for the owner, not as scope (G-07).

Sources: [Gestational Diabetes Tracker](https://apps.apple.com/us/app/gestational-diabetes-tracker/id1614657495) · [Malama](https://apps.apple.com/us/app/malama-gestational-diabetes/id1626320933) · [Ninta Health](https://nintahealth.com/) · [SNAQ, best GDM apps](https://www.snaq.ai/blog/best-gestational-diabetes-apps-2025) · [Healthline GDM food list](https://www.healthline.com/health/pregnancy/gestational-diabetes-food-list) · [UF gestational diabetes nutrition](https://diabetes.ufl.edu/outreach/resources/nutrition/gestational-diabetes-nutrition)

### 0A. Premise challenge

The plan is an honest implementation of PRD v1.1, and the PRD is blunt about its own risk (§11: "We are building before asking anyone"). The plan inherits six premises. It states two of them and assumes four.

| # | Premise | Stated? | Evaluation |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | The safety owner, and counsel where needed, will grant `gdm-organiser` on a useful timeline | Stated (§1) | **The long pole.** Every user-visible string waits on it, the working name and the landing included. §1 reads "Not started, drafted, not sent", and the same reviewers already hold the W-05 backlog of fifteen *live* rows. The plan wires the gate well and says nothing about its expected duration, who the reviewer is, or whether they have agreed to take 24 rows plus a new class. If this takes ten weeks, the first cohort of users has delivered. |
| P2 | Fifty door users arrive inside four weeks | **Assumed** | **The weakest premise, and the one the kill lines stand on.** The landing is `noindex`, D7 forbids spend, and rollout step 4 says "post the link in one place". No place is named. Below 50 every line is "unread", and an unread line never fires, so Tier 2 building continues on no evidence at all (G-02). |
| P3 | A pregnant user accepts a sign-in, a check-your-email page and a magic-link email that all say "Prediabetes Pal" | Stated (§8 item 10) | Plausibly wrong, and cheap to be wrong about. Her first contact after pressing "Sign up" is a brand for a condition she does not have, at the most anxious moment of the funnel, after a test in the same plan asserts the working name leads. Owner's call; the default stands, with a measurement added (G-04). |
| P4 | An organiser with no food guidance earns a sign-up and an explicit health-data consent | **Assumed** | The PRD doubts it itself ("the general answer may not be worth a door; eight of twelve rows resolve to write it down and bring it"). Against a notes app, the door's real additions are the plan's structure, the conflict flag and the printable page. That is a thin wedge, but it is the honest one available before S1–S3, and KL-T1 tests it directly. Accepted. |
| P5 | Sharing the app, the account and the database with the prediabetes door is an asset | Stated (D3) | **Verified better than expected.** Nudges key off `push_subscriptions`, trial crons off subscriptions, and error reporting is an allowlist with a scrubber, so a GDM-only account receives no prediabetes lifecycle traffic and her text does not reach Sentry. One real leak remains: "Erase my health data" on the GDM door calls the shared route and erases the *other* door's data too (G-05). |
| P6 | Building now, untested, is the right order (D6) | Stated, owner's decision | **Not re-litigated.** With build cost compressed, building Tier 1 is cheaper than the two-week hand-answered test it replaced; building *is* the test. That only holds if people arrive to take it, which returns to P2. The scarce resources here are reviewer attention and distribution, not engineering hours. |

**What happens if nothing is built.** She uses her notes app, the subreddit and a hospital PDF. The pain is real and the status quo is survivable. That argues for the smallest honest door, which is roughly what Tier 1 is.

**Reframing considered and not recommended:** a clinic-distributed version (the dietitian hands out the link with the referral, so the summary arrives filled in). It is the 12-month ideal (0C), it needs relationships the owner does not have today, and nothing in this plan forecloses it.

### 0B. Existing code leverage

| Sub-problem | Existing code it rides | New code |
|---|---|---|
| Surface-listed flag | `lib/guide-door-flag.ts` | `lib/gdm-door-flag.ts` (adds gates and a twin) |
| Production guard | `lib/guide-door-guard.ts` `ledgerStatuses` (reused, not copied) | `lib/gdm-door-guard.ts`, `docs/safety/gdm-gates.md` |
| Server twin | `lib/meal-memory-flag.ts`; the `twinMismatch` block in `next.config.ts` | Twin checked *inside* the guard (a list cannot be expressed as `=== "1"`) |
| Sign-up | `/signin` magic link with `callbackUrl` | None. Correct call. |
| Consent before storage | `app/api/check/route.ts` ("a signed-in session is not consent") | `app/api/gdm/profile` |
| Field encryption | `lib/server/crypto.ts` `encryptField` / `safeDecrypt` | None |
| Erase, export, cascade | Both account routes and their schema-derived tests | Two deletes, two export keys |
| Clinical routing | `lib/pal/clinical-risk.ts`, imported and never edited; approved `clinical-*` rows via `loadSafetyContract()` | `routeAboveFifth`; Tier 2 `routeGdmInput` |
| Copy governance | `scripts/validate-safety-contract.mjs` `getCopyLedgerRows`; `claims-boundary-copy.test.ts` `EXTRA_SOURCES` | `gdm-copy.test.ts`, which adds the ledger-to-source parity `DESIGN.md` §1.1 names as missing |
| Idea bank and its eval | `lib/pal/guide-ideas.ts`, `eval:pal:ideas` | Re-implemented, deliberately not imported |
| Print | `@media print` in `app/globals.css`; `window.print()` | One class |
| Model transport | `lib/pal/openai-client.ts` | Export-only touch (Tier 2) |

**Rebuilding anything?** Two deliberate duplications: the idea-rotation helper and the route-handler helpers (`closed()`, `json()`, the status shorthands, copied into three route files). The first is justified by the engine-isolation rule. The second is judged in Phase 3.

### 0C. Dream state

```
  CURRENT STATE                      THIS PLAN                           12-MONTH IDEAL
  The engine refuses every    --->   A second door on the same    --->   The appointment companion a
  pregnancy mention, on              app: an organiser with no            dietitian hands out with the
  purpose. A GDM user is             food content (Tier 1), food          referral. She arrives with one
  turned away with a row             features fully specified             page; the clinic sees fewer blank
  about A1C ranges. No GDM           behind S1-S5 (Tier 2).               first visits. Food ideas reviewed
  surface, table or class.           Dark until a class exists.           entry by entry. A read only
                                     Four kill lines wired.               against her own figure.
```

**Delta.** The plan builds the container and the governance and leaves the channel unbuilt. Everything in the 12-month column that is *code* is either in Tier 1 or specified in §5. What is missing is non-code: a named reviewer with a date (P1), and a named source for the first fifty users (P2). The plan moves toward the ideal and cannot reach it on engineering alone.

### 0C-bis. Implementation alternatives

```
APPROACH A: The plan as written
  Summary: PR-0 .. PR-6 dark behind one `organiser` surface, then Tier 2 modules as gates open.
  Effort:  L (human ~4-5 weeks / CC ~2-3 days)     Risk: Med
  Pros:    One ledger batch, as PRD §9 Step 2 asks. One release moment. Already fully specified.
  Cons:    First behavioural signal waits for all six features AND the class. The revert
           procedure does not work as documented (G-09). Unread kill lines default to "keep building".
  Reuses:  Everything in 0B.                        Completeness: 7/10

APPROACH B: Thin slice first (minimal viable)
  Summary: PR-0, PR-L, PR-1, then F-ASKLIST and F-SUMMARY only, on a per-feature surface. Hold
           F-PLANKEEP, F-WAIT and F-MYMEALS until the list shows use.
  Effort:  M (human ~2 weeks / CC ~1 day)           Risk: Low
  Pros:    Smallest batch for a scarce reviewer (about 12 rows, not 24). Earliest live signal.
           Least carrying cost if the door is closed.
  Cons:    Departs from PRD §9 Step 2's single batch. A questions list alone is nearly a notes
           app, so a weak result would be ambiguous. KL-T1's B arm (plan or meal) cannot be read.
  Reuses:  Same.                                    Completeness: 5/10

APPROACH C: The plan as written, with its defects closed (ideal)
  Summary: A, plus: a revert that works and is rehearsed where the guard runs (G-09); an
           "unread" rule that stops Tier 2 on no evidence (G-02); a signed-in end-to-end smoke
           (G-03); the honest holding line (G-06); precise erase copy (G-05); and whatever
           Phases 2-3 find.
  Effort:  L (A plus about half a day of CC)        Risk: Low-Med
  Pros:    Keeps the PRD's single batch. Every add is inside files the plan already touches.
  Cons:    Same time to first signal as A.
  Reuses:  Same.                                    Completeness: 9/10
```

**RECOMMENDATION: C** (P1 completeness, P2 every add is in the blast radius). Whether `organiser` should be split so the questions list can go live alone is the plan's own open question 4 and is genuinely close, so it goes to the final gate as a taste decision (G-01b), not decided here.

### 0D. Mode-specific analysis (SELECTIVE EXPANSION: hold scope first, then scan)

**Complexity check.** About 45 new files, two tables, three Tier 1 routes and a production guard. That trips the ">8 files" smell by a wide margin, and it is still the right size: this is a new product surface with its own consent path, not a feature. What keeps it honest is that four of the modules are pure (`plan-record`, `summary`, `waiting`, `onboarding`), one generic items table carries three lists, and nothing on the prediabetes door is edited. No reduction is proposed.

**Minimum set for the stated goal.** Everything in Tier 1 is named by PRD §6.2, so nothing may be cut by this review. One item could move later without blocking a kill line: Task 3.3, the sheet photo. No line measures it, and it carries the largest payloads in the door. It already sits last in PR-3; no change.

**Tier 2 sequencing, the one scope call worth making.** §5 allows building all of Tier 2 dark as soon as Tier 1 merges. Engineering hours are cheap here, and so are the evals (Task G1.3 is at least 36 ideas × 6 cells × 20 runs, about 4,300 small model calls: tens of dollars). What is not cheap: the S3 reviewer's entry-by-entry pass on a bank nobody has shown demand for, and the carrying cost of a fourth deep module, a second prompt and a metered route on a solo codebase. Recommendation: **do not start a §5 module until KL-T1 has been read** (not merely "has not fired"). Logged G-02.

**Expansion scan**

- *10x version.* The clinic-distributed companion in 0C. Not proposed: it is relationships, not code.
- *Platform potential.* `gdm_items` plus the per-kind zod registry is a small encrypted-notes platform. A third door could reuse it as it stands. No work needed now.
- *Delight candidates* (each about 30 minutes):
  1. The printed page carries the date it was printed, so a clinician can tell a stale sheet from a current one. *(Blocked by PRD §8 rule 5: a date she did not type is a number she did not type. Dropped.)*
  2. The printed page carries the door's name and address, opening the clinician channel. *(The wordmark already prints. An address would read `prediabetespal.com/gdm`, which collides with D7 and P3. Deferred, G-07.)*
  3. After she parks a question, focus returns to the field so the next one is one keystroke away. *(Approved into the Design phase as an interaction-state item.)*
  4. The holding page tells the truth about what happens next. *(Approved, G-06.)*
  5. "Add to my questions" from a plan conflict confirms in place rather than navigating away. *(Design phase.)*
  6. Keep an unsent *plan* draft on the device as the ask draft is kept, since a plan is the longest thing she types. *(Rejected: a figure is health data, and Global Constraints say nothing health-related is stored on-device. The ask draft is already the edge of that rule.)*

### 0E. Temporal interrogation

  HOUR 1 (foundations, PR-0)   Line numbers are stale on arrival: the ledger and globals.css move weekly.
                               Anchor on the named symbol, never the number. The twin-inside-the-guard is
                               new; the existing twin tests' PAIRS table cannot express it (the plan says so).
  HOUR 2-3 (core, PR-1..3)     Tasks 1.1-1.3 must land as one commit or `account-export` is red in between
                               (the plan says so, twice, in different words: pick one). SessionInfo's

                               `plan_photo` is a DB kind and deliberately not a GdmItemKind: the executor
                               will think that is a bug unless told.

                               calls it needs the file traced into its function bundle. The plan checks this

                               gdm-copy's JSX-text regex will false-positive on `a > b && <X/>`.
  HOUR 6+ (polish, release)    The revert rehearsal on a preview proves nothing: the guard that blocks the

### 0F. Mode

SELECTIVE EXPANSION (autoplan override). Scope held; seven candidates surfaced; five accepted, two deferred; one taste decision raised. Approach C. (G-01)

### Section 1 — Architecture

```
                 BUILD TIME (production only)                              RUN TIME
  docs/safety/copy-ledger.md ─┐                                   GDM_DOOR_ENABLED (server twin)
  docs/safety/gdm-gates.md ───┼─▶ lib/gdm-door-guard.ts ─▶ next.config.ts: throw | effective list
  NEXT_PUBLIC_GDM_DOOR ───────┘      ▲ reuses ledgerStatuses()              │ inlined into every bundle
                                     │ from lib/guide-door-guard.ts          ▼
                                                              lib/gdm-door-flag.ts
  app/gdm/layout.tsx  (frame; build flag only)                gdmDoorEnabled(surface) · gdmDoorServerEnabled()
    ├─ page.tsx             landing              [landing]
    ├─ start/page.tsx       holding | onboarding | → home
    ├─ privacy/page.tsx                          [organiser]
    └─ (door)/layout.tsx    nav only. Every page: requireGdmDoor() ─▶ lib/server/gdm-door.ts ─▶ session + gdm_profiles
         home · questions · plan · meals · summary · data
              │  components/gdm/* (client)  ── every string from lib/pal/gdm/copy.ts (GDM_COPY)
              ▼  lib/client/gdm-api.ts (gdmFetch: JSON in and out, never throws)
  app/api/gdm/profile      consent + appointment date ──┐
  app/api/gdm/items        ask | plan | meal ───────────┼─▶ encryptField ─▶ Postgres: gdm_profiles, gdm_items
  app/api/gdm/plan-photo   plan_photo (base64) ─────────┘
       │ ask text ─▶ lib/pal/gdm/asklist.ts ─▶ lib/pal/clinical-risk.ts   (imported, never edited)
       │                                   └─▶ loadSafetyContract()       (reads the ledger from disk AT RUN TIME)
  SHARED, door-agnostic:   /api/account/export · DELETE /api/account/health-data · /api/account/delete
  NEVER imported by GDM:   lib/pal/{service,labels,a1c,prompt,boundary-copy}   (pinned by gdm-pregnancy-route.test)
  TIER 2 (dark):           router.ts ─▶ plan-read.ts (deterministic) ─▶ plan-read-model.ts ─▶ openai-client (export-only)
```

**Boundaries.** Sound. The door is a vertical slice with one inbound seam (the shared account and its three account routes) and one outbound seam (the clinical router and the ledger). Four modules are pure. The engine-isolation rule is enforced by a test, not by intention.

**The one flow worth tracing on all four paths: parking a question.**

```
 her text ─▶ gdmFetch ─▶ closed? ─▶ session? ─▶ consent? ─▶ zod ─▶ cap? ─▶ encrypt ─▶ INSERT ─▶ route + routeCopy ─▶ 200
    │           │          │404       │401        │403       │400    │409     │throw     │throw     │throw
  empty      offline    kill       expired     erased in   too     200      key        DB         ledger unreadable
  (button    save-      switch     "try        another     long    rows     missing    down       ⇒ 500 AFTER the row is
  state      failed,    save-      again"      tab:        "try    "try     ⇒ 500      ⇒ 500      stored. She retries, gets
  never      draft      failed     ✗ never     consent     again"  again"   calm line, calm       a duplicate, and never sees
  specified) kept ✓     ✓          works       line ✓      ✗       ✗        Sentry ✓   line ✓     the urgent card  ✗✗
```

Three of those columns tell her to "try again in a moment" when trying again can never work (401, 400, 409). One fails at the single most safety-relevant moment in Tier 1. Decisions:

- **G-11 · Compute the clinical route before the insert, and load the contract on every ask, not only on a hit.** As written, `loadSafetyContract()` runs only when one of the four routes fires, which is the rarest and most serious input the list will ever see, and it runs *after* the row is stored. If the ledger is missing from the function bundle, or malformed, the first time anyone learns it is when a woman types that she is shaky and clammy and gets "That did not save just now". Reordering costs two lines, makes a contract failure loud on the first ordinary question, and makes a retry safe. Whether the new routes need a file-tracing entry is settled with evidence in Phase 3. *Mechanical. P1, P5.*
- **G-14 · An error must not give advice that cannot work.** `gdmFetch` maps 401 to a redirect to `GDM_ROUTES.signup` (the ask draft survives by design). Every text input carries a `maxLength` equal to its zod bound, so a 400 is unreachable in normal use. A 409 gets its own row (`gdm-list-full`, one sentence, `Pending`). `DESIGN.md` §2: "Errors say what to do next." *Mechanical. P1.*

**State machines.**

```
 DOOR (derived per request)                   PLAN ENTRY                      WAITING MODE (derived, never stored)
 closed ──flag + twin──▶ signed_out           (none) ──save──▶ current         waiting ──date passes──▶ after_appointment
   ▲                        │ magic link                        │ replace          │ plan saved             │ plan saved
   │ kill switch            ▼                                   ▼                  ▼                        ▼
   └─────────────────── no_profile ──consent──▶ open         replaced            ended ◀────────────────────┘
                            ▲                     │           (kept, dated)
                            └────── erase ────────┘           no edit, no delete, no un-replace
```

No invalid transition is reachable: `open` requires a `gdm_profiles` row and only the consent branch writes one. One *missing* transition matters: a plan entry can never be removed or corrected, only replaced, and the replace is two client calls (POST the new, then PATCH the old). When the PATCH fails she holds two current plans, the plan calls that "recoverable", and nothing on the surface recovers it: pressing Replace again creates a third.

- **G-13 · Make Replace one server operation.** `POST /api/gdm/items` accepts an optional `replaces: <uuid>` for `kind: "plan"` and does the insert and the `replacedOn` update in one transaction, scoped by `userId`. Ten lines, one test, and the two-current-plans state stops existing instead of being documented. *Mechanical. P5 (explicit over clever), P1.*

**Coupling.** New and justified: the two shared account routes import the GDM schema (correct direction). New and slightly odd: door B's guard imports door A's parser (`ledgerStatuses` from `lib/guide-door-guard.ts`). It is the DRY choice and it is pure, so it stands; if a third door arrives, move the parser to a neutral module. `gateStatuses` is the repo's **third** markdown-table parser (after `ledgerStatuses` and `getCopyLedgerRows`). Noted as debt in Section 10, not changed.

**Scaling and single points of failure.** Nothing here is load-sensitive at any plausible size. What bends first is the sheet photo: each view decrypts about 3.7 MB of ciphertext with `no-store`, and the account export inlines up to three of them (Phase 3, performance). Single points of failure are inherited, not added: `HEALTH_DATA_KEY`, the ledger on disk, the magic-link sender.

**Rollback posture.** Broken as documented. See Section 9, G-09.

### Section 2 — Error & Rescue Map

Route handlers here do not raise typed exceptions, so "exception class" is read as *the named failure*. `gdmFetch` never throws, so every unrescued server throw reaches her as one calm line. That design is good. The gaps are where the calm line is untrue, or where a failure reaches nobody.

| Codepath | What goes wrong | Rescued? | Rescue action | She sees | Tested? |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `POST /api/gdm/profile` | Body is not JSON / fails zod | Y | 400 | `gdm-save-failed` | Y |
| | Consent false | Y | 400 + the approved consent line | The consent line | Y |
| | `encryptField` throws (key missing or short) | N | 500 → Sentry (scrubbed allowlist, verified) | `gdm-save-failed` | N, acceptable |
| | Second POST (double-tap on "Agree and continue") | Y | `onConflictDoUpdate` **overwrites `consentedAt`** | Nothing | N → **G-22** |
| `GET /api/gdm/profile` | Date ciphertext unreadable | Y | Returns the `(unreadable entry)` placeholder as the date | Empty date field; phrase "none" | Y |
| `POST /api/gdm/items` | Session expired | Y | 401 | "Try again in a moment" ✗ never works | status only → **G-14** |
| | Text over the bound | Y | 400 | same ✗ | status only → **G-14** |
| | Over the cap | Y | 409 | same ✗ | status only → **G-14** |
| | Contract unreadable on a clinical hit | **N** | 500 after the row is stored | save-failed; duplicate on retry; **no urgent card** | **N → G-11** |
| `listGdmItems` | A row will not decrypt or parse | Swallowed | Row dropped, **nothing logged** | An entry silently vanishes; it still counts toward the cap | **N → G-12, critical gap** |
| Replace (client) | PATCH fails after POST succeeded | N | None specified | Two current plans, no control to retire one | N → **G-13** |
| `POST /api/gdm/plan-photo` | Body far over the limit | Partly | Length checked only after the whole body is parsed | save-failed | Y → **G-20** |
| | `createImageBitmap` rejects (HEIC on some browsers) | **Unspecified** | — | **Unspecified** | N → **G-20** |
| `GET /api/gdm/plan-photo` | Ciphertext unreadable | N | 500 | A broken-image icon | N, low |
| Account export | An unreadable entry | Y | Exports the placeholder string | The placeholder | Y, by design |
| Any page under `app/gdm/(door)/` | DB down inside `requireGdmDoor()` | **N** | No `error.tsx` exists anywhere under `app/` | Next's raw "Application error" page | **N → G-15** |
| `window.print()` | Host is an in-app browser where print is a no-op | N | None | She taps Print and nothing happens | N → **G-18** |

- **G-12 · A dropped row must reach somebody.** `listGdmItems` returns `{ items, unreadable: number }` and reports a count with the kind, never content, when it is non-zero. A vanished question is the door's version of data loss, and as written neither she nor the owner would ever know. By the registry's rule (rescued by swallowing, untested, silent) this is the review's one **critical gap**. *Mechanical. P1.*
- **G-15 · A calm error boundary for the door.** `app/gdm/error.tsx` (client component, `reset()` wired to a button) plus one row, `gdm-load-failed` (`line`: That did not load just now. · `retry`: Try again). Without it an anxious user meets an unstyled stack-trace-shaped page. `DESIGN.md` §12: "no paid or signed-in user is ever dead-ended." *Mechanical. P1. In blast radius: `app/gdm/`, the bank, the ledger.*
- **G-22 · Keep the first consent time.** `onConflictDoUpdate` sets only `appointmentCiphertext`; `consentedAt` is written once. The timestamp is the record that explicit consent was given, and a double-tap should not move it. *Mechanical. P5.*

### Section 9 — Deployment and rollout

**G-09 · CRITICAL · The documented kill switch cannot be pulled.** §7.3 step 6 reads: "unset `GDM_DOOR_ENABLED` and redeploy — an env flip; every `/api/gdm/*` route and every signed-in door page 404s at once. Then unset `NEXT_PUBLIC_GDM_DOOR` in a reviewed rebuild." Task 0.2's guard refuses exactly that state: surfaces listed and twin not `1` is an error, and `next.config.ts` throws on any error when `VERCEL_ENV=production`. On Vercel an environment change takes effect only in a new deployment, and a new deployment runs `next build`. So the first step of the revert fails to build, and the previous deployment, door open, keeps serving health-data routes during the incident the step exists for.

Three things make this worse than a wording slip:

1. **The plan's own test pins the failure.** Task 0.2 Step 5 asserts that a listed surface without the twin "fails the production build on the twin".
2. **The rehearsal cannot catch it.** §4 step 5 rehearses the revert on a preview, where `VERCEL_ENV` is `preview` and the guard never runs. The rehearsal passes and proves nothing.
3. **The name invites the mistake.** Under stress an operator unsets the variable called the kill switch. That is the one move that leaves the door open.

Verified against the tree: the four existing twins behave the same way (`next.config.ts:49-74` throws on a client `1` with the twin unset), so "runtime kill switch" in this repo has always meant "unset both". This plan is the first to write a procedure that unsets only one. Note that `launch-controls.md` §14.1, in the same plan, already says "unset `NEXT_PUBLIC_GDM_DOOR` and `GDM_DOOR_ENABLED`, redeploy": the plan contradicts itself, and §14.1 is the version that works.

Two ways to fix it. Both are small; they differ in what happens when a human gets it half right.

- **Option A, fail closed (recommended).** In `checkProductionGdmDoor`, a twin that is not `1` closes every surface instead of erroring: `{ effective: "", errors: [] }` plus a build-log warning, the same posture the guide guard already takes for stale idea labels. Either variable alone then closes the door, the landing included. A forgotten twin on first release yields a dark door and a warning rather than a failed build; §7.3 step 5's first-minute check catches it.
- **Option B, document the truth.** Keep the guard strict. Rewrite §7.3 step 6, the `GDM_DOOR_ENABLED` row in `env-reference.md` and the docstring in `lib/gdm-door-flag.ts` to say "unset **both**, redeploy", and drop "without a rebuild", which is not a thing on Vercel.

Either way: add the revert to `scripts/check-production-config.ts` as a case, so it is rehearsed where the guard actually runs. Recommendation A, because on a health-data door the incident lever should be impossible to pull wrong. It costs a second twin semantic in one `next.config.ts`, which a comment must explain. **Both are viable, so this goes to the final gate as a taste decision.** That *something* must change is not in question.

**G-10 · HIGH · PR-1 is the one dark merge that is not safe to merge.** The plan has the order right (§1 and §2.1: migration `0020` before PR-1 deploys) and never says why it matters. `GET /api/account/export` and `DELETE /api/account/health-data` deliberately ignore the GDM flag and, from PR-1 on, query `gdm_profiles` and `gdm_items` unconditionally. If `main` deploys before the migration is applied, **export and erase return 500 for every existing prediabetes user**, on the two routes that carry legal rights, while the door they belong to is dark and nobody is looking. CI checks schema drift, not whether production has applied a migration, and the build is plain `next build`. Amendments: state the blast radius in the PR-1 row and in Task 1.1's release note; add a pre-merge check the owner can run in ten seconds (`select to_regclass('public.gdm_items')` is not null); add "export and erase as a test user" to the first five minutes after PR-1 deploys. *Mechanical. P1.*

**Everything else in this section is in good shape.** The migration is additive (two `CREATE TABLE`, no lock on an existing table, backward compatible in the safe direction). Both CI legs exist and the flag-off leg's name is preserved as the required check. The production config check covers three refusal cases. §7.3 step 5 is a real first-five-minutes list. Retiring the flag is planned (§7.3 step 8), which most plans forget.

```
 DEPLOY ORDER                                         REVERT (as it must read after G-09)
 1 owner applies 0020 ── verify to_regclass           A: unset EITHER variable ─▶ redeploy ─▶ door dark
 2 merge PR-0 … PR-6, both vars unset (dark)          B: unset BOTH variables ─▶ redeploy ─▶ door dark
 3 class exists + landing rows Approved               then: /api/health gdmDoor all "off"; export + erase still 200
     ─▶ twin=1, flag=landing                          never: unset the twin alone under the guard as written
 4 batch G1 Approved + counsel read the notice              (build fails, old deployment keeps serving)
     ─▶ flag=landing,organiser  ── clocks start
 5 first five minutes: §7.3 step 5
```

### Section 4 — Data flow and interaction edge cases

The plan specifies what each surface contains and almost nothing about what it does while something is happening. No form in §2.3 has a specified saving, disabled or in-flight state.

| Interaction | Edge case | Handled? | How, or the fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| Any Save / Add / Agree button | Double-tap | **No** | Two POSTs give two identical rows. For plans that is two identical current cards with no delete control. **G-26:** every submit disables itself while in flight and shows its state in an `aria-live="polite"` region (`DESIGN.md` §12). No new copy is needed if the label simply does not change |
| Add a question | Connection drops | Yes | Draft kept in `pal.gdm.ask.draft`, cleared on a 200 |
| Enter a plan (the longest thing she types) | Connection drops, or session expired | **No** | The form is lost. It cannot be drafted on the device (a figure is health data; Global Constraints). **G-26:** on any failed save the form keeps its values in memory and stays on screen. Say so in Task 3.2; today only the ask list says it |
| Any list | Two tabs or two devices | Partly | Row-level writes, so nothing is overwritten. Nothing refetches, so a list is stale until reload. Accepted at this scale |
| Any list | Zero items | Partly | "Nothing here yet." See Phase 2: that is `DESIGN.md` §12's banned empty state |
| Any list | 200 items | Yes | 409, with untrue advice (G-14) |
| Erase my health data | She also has a prediabetes profile | **No** | The shared route erases both doors. `eraseWarn` says "everything you have kept here". **G-05** |
| Erase, then Back | Cached door page | Yes | Pages are dynamic and re-run `requireGdmDoor()` → `/gdm/start` |
| Sign-up | She taps the magic link in a mail app while `/gdm` was open in an in-app browser | **No** | The session lands in a different browser from the one she was using. Pre-existing and app-wide (`lib/client/attribution.ts` is the only in-app-browser code), but this door's launch plan is one social post, so most first visitors will be in exactly that state. **G-18** |
| Summary → Print | In-app browser | **No** | `window.print()` is a no-op in several. **G-18** |
| Sheet photo | 12 MB HEIC from an iPhone | **Unspecified** | **G-20** |
| Appointment date | A date in the past | Yes | `appointmentPhrase` returns `none`; waiting mode moves to `after_appointment` |
| Kill switch mid-session | She presses Save | Yes | 404 → the calm line, and this time it is true |

- **G-18 · In-app browsers are this door's front door.** Deferred to TODOS.md for the fix (the sign-in page is never-edited), **accepted now** for the measurement: G-04's landing-to-`told` read is where this loss will show, and §14.4 should say that a poor ratio means "check the in-app browser path before concluding nobody wants it". For print, the summary page gets one bank line under the button for the no-op case only if the owner wants a new row; otherwise the page is already readable on screen and she can screenshot it. *P3 for the fix (outside blast radius), P1 for the read.*
- **G-20 · The photo path needs three lines of hardening and one state.** Reject on `Content-Length` before `request.json()`; send `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` on the GET; `loading="lazy"` on the `<img>`. And specify what she sees when the device cannot decode the file: the `gdm-save-failed` line, the file input cleared. *Mechanical. P1.*

### Section 5 — Code quality

- **G-16 · Four copies of a security-critical guard.** Task 2.1 says of the route helpers "copy them; two files do not earn a shared module". By the end of Tier 1 it is three files, and four with `/api/gdm/read`. What is being copied is not boilerplate: it is the closed → session → consent order, and the consent `SELECT`. Three chances to get that order wrong is two too many. Extract `lib/server/gdm-route.ts`: `gdmRouteGuard(deps): Promise<{ userId: string } | Response>`, about twenty lines, tested once, and each route test keeps its own three status assertions. *Mechanical. P4 (DRY), P5.*
- **Naming.** `routeAboveFifth` is named for how it works (a position in an array), not what it answers. `askListCardRoute` says it. The order is pinned by a test, so this is a nit. *Accepted as a rename; no behaviour change.*
- **The medication-field scan cannot see what it says it sees.** `gdm-a1c-free.test.ts` looks for `medication…[:=?]`. It catches a declared field and would not catch `z.object({ meds_taken: … })` nested in a record or a key built by concatenation. The strict zod schemas are the real guard and they are good. The test is a tripwire, not a fence; its title should say so. *Nit, noted.*
- **Over-engineering check.** None found. `GDM_SURFACE_GATES` for Tier 2 looks early in PR-0 and is not: the guard must refuse `ideas` and `read` from the first day it exists.
- **Under-engineering check.** No `maxLength` on inputs (G-14). No in-flight state on any form (G-26). The cap and the three-photo limit are count-then-insert and can be exceeded by concurrent requests; both are anti-abuse bounds, not invariants, so a one-line `ponytail:` comment naming the race is the right size of fix.
- **Branching.** No new function branches more than five times. `waitingState`, `appointmentPhrase` and `nextGdmStep` are tables. Good.

### Section 7 — Performance

Nothing here is hot. Three notes, all small.

- The summary page makes three `listGdmItems` calls. Run them with `Promise.all`. *Accepted.*
- The index `(user_id, kind, created_at)` covers every list query and the cap's `COUNT`. No N+1: each list is one query and decryption is in-process.
- The sheet photo is the only heavy object. A 2 MB JPEG becomes 2.8 MB of base64, then about 3.7 MB of ciphertext in one `text` cell. Every view decrypts it again, because the GET is `no-store`. That is the right privacy choice on a shared device and costs a second or two on mobile data. `loading="lazy"` (G-20) is enough. The export inlining three of them is judged in Phase 3 against the response-size evidence.

### Section 8 — Observability

The product metrics are the best-specified part of the plan: closed enums, stated numerators and denominators, a sample floor, and a database cross-check for the day analytics goes blind. The operational side is nearly empty.

| Question an operator will ask | Can they answer it today? |
|---|---|
| Is the door up? | Partly. `/api/health` reports flags, not whether `/api/gdm/*` can reach the database or the ledger |
| Did a save fail for anyone today? | Only if it threw. Sentry sees a 500 (allowlist and scrubber verified). Rows dropped by `listGdmItems` reach nobody (G-12) |
| Is analytics blind? | Yes, by the §14.1 cross-check. Good |
| Why are there sign-ups and no door users? | No. Nothing separates "did not want it" from "the magic link opened in another browser" (G-18) |
| A kill line fired. What do I do? | §7.3 step 6, which does not work (G-09) |

No new dashboard or alert is warranted at fifty users. Two cheap additions are: G-12's count, and one sentence in §14 telling the reader to look at Sentry for `/api/gdm/` errors before reading any line as "nobody used it". *Accepted into G-02's §14 amendment.*

### Section 10 — Long-term trajectory

- **Reversibility: 4 of 5.** One flag, isolated directories, two additive tables, no edit to the first door. The parts that do not reverse are not code: stored pregnancy-related data and the consent records for it; any sentence a user has already read; a claim class once counsel has granted it.
- **Debt this plan adds.** (1) About fifteen source-scan tests that assert on the text of a file rather than on behaviour. It is the repo's pattern under `environment: "node"`, and each one is a future false alarm. (2) A third markdown-table parser. (3) A second copy of the idea-rotation helper, deliberately. (4) Up to four copies of the route guard unless G-16 lands.
- **Path dependency.** Low. `gdm_items` with a per-kind zod registry absorbs new list types without a migration. D3's split triggers are named, and F-PLANREAD is correctly flagged as the first.
- **Knowledge concentration.** The plan is its own documentation and is unusually complete. The risk runs the other way: at 3,400 lines, an executor will not hold the Global Constraints in mind by Task 5. Each task card already restates its copy and flag rule; that is the right mitigation.
- **The one-year read.** A new engineer opening `lib/pal/gdm/` in a year finds pure modules with tests and a guard whose errors name their fix. They will not find out from the code why the door has no food content, or why nobody may edit a `Status` cell. `docs/safety/gdm-gates.md` says it. Good.
- **What comes after.** If KL-T1 is survived: ideas, then the read, as specified. If it is not: the door closes with one redeploy, the data stays exportable and erasable, and the codebase carries two dormant tables. That is an acceptable worst case, which is the strongest thing to be said for the whole plan.

### Section 3 — Security and threat model

New attack surface: three authenticated JSON routes in Tier 1, one metered model route in Tier 2, no new secret, no new dependency, no background job. Every claim in the "Mitigated?" column was checked against the tree.

| Threat | Likelihood | Impact | Mitigated? |
|---|---|---|---|
| User A reads, edits or deletes user B's item or photo by id | Low | High | **Yes.** Every query is scoped by `userId`, and both route tests try another user's id |
| Cross-site request on POST / PATCH / DELETE | Low | Med | **Inherited.** No origin-check helper exists anywhere in the repo and `auth.ts` sets no cookie override, so the door relies on Auth.js's default `SameSite=Lax`, exactly as `/api/profile` does. Consistent; accepted |
| A database leak exposes stored pregnancy-related data | Low | **Very high** | **Partly.** Bodies and the date are field-encrypted. A `gdm_profiles` row joined to `users.email` still names a pregnant person in plaintext. That is unavoidable for the feature, and it is why counsel's read and G-08 (expiry) matter more here than on the first door |
| Her text reaches a third party | Low | High | **Yes.** Analytics are closed enums under a source scan; Sentry is an allowlist with `sendDefaultPii: false` and a scrubber; Umami drops query strings. One correction for §8 item 16: Umami still records the **path**, so renaming `gdm_*` events would not hide the door from the vendor while pages live under `/gdm/` |
| Oversized or mislabelled upload | Low | Low | Partly → G-20 |
| Storage or CPU abuse through the unmetered Tier 1 routes | Low | Low | **Bounded, not limited.** The proxy matcher covers six paths and none is `/api/gdm/*`. Rows are capped per account, and account creation is rate-limited at `/api/auth/*`. Churn is unbounded. Accepted, with the ceiling named in the `ponytail:` comment the plan already writes |
| Prompt injection (Tier 2) | Low | Low–Med | **Structural.** The model sees a description and an occasion, returns two numbers, and authors no text. The worst case is a skewed estimate for the person who typed it. See G-29 |
| A gate opened by editing a `Status` cell | Low | High | **Overstated in the plan.** See G-31 |

- **G-31 · The build enforces the state of a gate, not who set it.** The plan's opening rule says an executor never edits a `Status` cell and that "the production build enforces all three mechanically". It enforces that the cell *reads* `Signed`. It cannot know who typed it. The real control is the owner reading the diff. Two amendments: correct that sentence, and pin the four gate statuses in `gdm-door-guard.test.ts` against the real file, so signing a gate is a two-file change no reviewer can miss. *Mechanical. P5.*
- **For counsel's packet** (the privacy-notice row in §1 already routes there; these are the questions to put): Washington's My Health My Data Act and its consumer-health-data policy and consent requirements; the FTC Health Breach Notification Rule; state law on reproductive-health data; whether the notice must say how long data is kept (it does not today; G-08).

### Section 6 — Test review (CEO altitude; the coverage diagram is in Phase 3)

The pure modules are tested the way they should be: behaviour, edges, and a negative control per regex family so a pattern that stops biting fails loudly. The route tests run real SQL in PGlite and check the ciphertext at rest. That is better than most plans.

The pyramid is lopsided in one direction. About fifteen tests assert on the *text of a source file* because the suite runs without a DOM, and exactly one smoke spec covers a signed-out landing. **Nothing automated ever signs in and uses the door.** The consent checkbox being unchecked by default, the single most legally loaded pixel here, is verified by a person with a checklist (Task 1.5 Step 3). G-03 closes that. The 2 a.m. Friday test for this door is: sign in, consent, park a question, enter two differing plans, open the summary, erase, and confirm the door forgets her. Phase 3 writes it down.

Evals: Tier 1 has no model call, so none apply. Tier 2's two live evals are correctly owner-run, keyed on prompt version and model id, and gated out of CI.

### Section 11 — Design and UX (CEO altitude; seven passes follow in Phase 2)

```
 one post ─▶ /gdm landing ──"Sign up"──▶ /signin   "Prediabetes Pal account … keeps your history and coach in sync"   ← P3
                                            │ magic link opens in the DEFAULT browser, not the in-app one             ← G-18
                                            ▼
                                       /gdm/start ── organiser dark ──▶ holding line; she can never be told          ← G-06
                                            │ organiser on
                                            ▼
                     told? ──not yet──▶ one fixed line (an exit, counted, not chased)
                       │ yes
                       ▼
                     date (optional) ─▶ consent (unchecked) ─▶ POST profile ─▶ /gdm/home
                                                                                  │
                  ┌───────────────── waiting ◀────────────────────────────────────┤ plan entered
                  │ ACOG line · checklist · appointment · three links              ▼
                  ▼                                                     ended: a title and four links   ← G-32
        questions ◀── "Add a question", on every screen
        plan ── two entries differ ──▶ park the differ question
        meals ── include in my summary
        summary ── Print ──▶ the page she carries in                     ← the peak moment; G-32 hides its invitation
        data ── download · erase (both doors: G-05) · delete · sign out
```

- **G-30 · HIGH · The door wears the first door's clothes everywhere the plan did not look.** The landing test asserts the page component never says "Prediabetes Pal". The root layout says it for every route: `openGraph.siteName`, `appleWebApp.title`, a root `app/opengraph-image.tsx`, and a manifest named "Prediabetes Pal" whose `start_url` is `/home`. So the link preview of the one post meant to bring the first fifty users shows the first door's card and name; "Add to Home Screen" from `/gdm/home` installs an app called Prediabetes Pal; and that app opens on the prediabetes dashboard, which PRD §7.1 says this profile must never reach. Fix, all inside `app/gdm/`: the layout exports `metadata` (description from the bank's hero line, `openGraph.siteName` and `appleWebApp.title` from `gdm-door-name`), a plain `opengraph-image.tsx` that renders the working name from the bank, and a manifest served by a route handler that builds it from `GDM_COPY` and 404s while the door is dark (a static file in `public/` would publish the working name before the class exists). *Mechanical. P1, P2.*
- **G-32 · HIGH · Entering a plan deletes her appointment.** In `ended`, Home becomes "`planTitle` and a link … with the three links below". The appointment phrase, the control that changes the date, and the "your summary is ready to print" line all live only in the waiting states. A woman who types in her obstetrician's handout before she has seen the dietitian, which is PRD row 12's own scenario, loses the countdown, can no longer correct her date anywhere on the door, and is never offered the summary she is now best placed to print. The plan follows PRD §6.2 to the letter ("the mode ends on its own when a plan is entered"), so the gap is the PRD's. Recommendation: the appointment block is a fixture of Home in all three states; what ends when a plan arrives is the ACOG line and the checklist. **Because this reads PRD wording more narrowly than written, it goes to the final gate.**
- The rest (no interaction states on any form, empty states that `DESIGN.md` §12 bans by name, a seven-item nav with no pattern at 375 px, focus management on every add and delete) is Phase 2's work.

### NOT in scope (Phase 1)

| Considered | Why it is not in this plan |
|---|---|
| A "tell me when it opens" email for landing-only sign-ups (G-06) | Needs a template, a send path and an opt-in line: new infrastructure. The honest holding line is in; the email is a TODO |
| The printed summary as a clinician-facing channel (G-07) | An address on paper reads `prediabetespal.com/gdm`. Waits on D7 |
| Automatic expiry of GDM data (G-08) | A new cron. Goes to counsel with the privacy notice |
| Fixing sign-in for in-app browsers (G-18) | The sign-in page is never-edited by this plan, and the problem is app-wide |
| A `/gdm/signin` page, check-email page and second email template (§8 item 10's alternative) | `pages.verifyRequest` is global in `auth.ts`; a second flow means per-request logic in shared auth. Owner's call, surfaced at the final gate |
| Rate limits on the Tier 1 routes | Bounded by per-account caps and the limit on `/api/auth/*`. `proxy.ts` is Tier 2 only by the plan's own rule |
| Waiting-mode content on the public landing, before the sign-in wall | Moves organiser copy onto a public page and nearer to food content. Revisit only if G-04's ratio is poor |
| A feedback box for the tone measure | A free-text surface for health data. The plan says so in §7.4 and is right |
| The clinic-distributed version | The 12-month ideal. Relationships, not code |
| Everything in the plan's own §3 and §6 | Already reasoned there; nothing in this review reopens it |

### Failure Modes Registry

A row that is unrescued, untested and silent is a **critical gap**.

| Codepath | Failure mode | Rescued? | Test? | She sees | Logged? | |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Revert by the twin alone (§7.3 step 6) | Production build throws; the open door keeps serving | N | Pinned as *expected* by Task 0.2 | Nothing. The operator sees a failed deploy | Build log | **CRITICAL** · G-09 |
| `listGdmItems`, a row that will not decrypt or parse | Dropped | Swallowed | N | **Silent** | **N** | **CRITICAL GAP** · G-12 |
| PR-1 deployed before migration `0020` | Export and erase 500 for every user | N | N | A generic error on a legal-rights route | Sentry | HIGH · G-10 |
| Link preview, home-screen install | The first door's name and card; the installed app opens `/home` | N | N (the test reads page text only) | The wrong product | N | HIGH · G-30 |
| A plan entered before the appointment | The appointment block disappears | N/A | Pinned as *expected* (`ended`) | No countdown, no way to edit the date, no summary offer | N | HIGH · G-32 |
| A clinical hit while the contract is unreadable | 500 after the row is stored | N | N | The calm line; no urgent card; a duplicate on retry | Sentry | G-11 |
| Replace, second call fails | Two current plans | N | N | Two cards, no way to retire one | N | G-13 |
| Save with an expired session, an over-long text, or a full list | Untrue advice | Y | Status only | "Try again in a moment" | N | G-14 |
| Database down on a door page | No error boundary under `app/` | N | N | Next's raw error page | Sentry | G-15 |
| Double-tap on any submit | Duplicate rows | N | N | Duplicates; plans cannot be deleted | N | G-26 |
| Fewer than fifty users | Every line unread; Tier 2 proceeds | N | N | — | — | G-02 |
| Arriving in an in-app browser | The magic link opens elsewhere; print does nothing | N | N | "Nothing happened" | N | G-18 |
| Tier 2: a wide model estimate | The range overlaps any figure, so the read says "about" | N | N | False reassurance | N | G-29 (Phase 3) |

Codex: unavailable (usage limit until 2026-10-14). Claude subagent (`ceo-voice`, fresh context, no sight of this review): launched at the start of Phase 1, sent a time-box instruction at 23 minutes, **had not reported by the final gate.** The premise gate was therefore asked on the primary review alone, as the skill's degradation rule allows ("outside voices unavailable, continuing with primary review"). `[single-reviewer]`. If the voice reports later, anything it raises that is not already here is a candidate taste decision for a re-run.

*(Subagent column filled in after the final gate; see the correction at the top of this record and the Post-gate addendum. The voice read all 3,470 lines and finished at 04:39Z, before this record's first word was written, so it never saw the primary review. Zero of six confirmed. The primary review was the more forgiving of the two on every dimension.)*

The six-month regret, stated once: the door ships, a dozen people find it, no line is ever readable, the reviewer's queue has absorbed thirty-five new rows, and Tier 2 was built anyway. G-02, G-33's two rows and G-04 are aimed at exactly that.

## Phase 2 — Design review (UI scope detected; all seven passes; auto-decided)

### Step 0 — Design scope

**Initial rating: 5/10.** The plan is a 10 on *what she reads*: every label and sentence is drafted, banked, tested and traceable to a ledger row, which almost no plan does. It is a 3 on *what the screen does*. No form has a saving state. No list has a loading state. Home's content after its main event (a plan arrives) is a title and four links. The navigation has seven entries and no pattern. A 10 for this plan is: a first-second-third hierarchy per screen, one state table, a decided nav at 375 px, and focus rules for every add, delete and step change, all inside `DESIGN.md`'s existing tokens and with no new visual language.

**DESIGN.md:** present, 609 lines, and modified in the working tree (it is never-edited by this plan; the working copy was read). Every decision below is calibrated against it. **Mockups:** none; no `design` binary on this machine.

**Existing leverage the plan already uses well:** `page-shell` / `page-frame` / `surface-card` / `primary-button` / `legal-card` / `app-skip`; the `app/guides/layout.tsx` standalone pattern; native `<input type="date">` and `window.print()` over libraries; the existing `@media print` block. **Leverage it misses:** `.chip-row` / `.selectable-chip` (§10) for the nav; §12's `aria-live="polite"` rule; §12's `role="list"` rule; §12's `break-inside: avoid` for print rows; rail 9's 56 px marketing CTA.

**Classifier:** HYBRID. `/gdm` is a marketing surface (rail 15); everything under `(door)/` is app UI.

### Pass 1 — Information architecture · 5/10 → 8/10

The plan lists each screen's parts and rarely their rank. What she should meet first, second and third:

| Screen | First | Second | Third | Change from the plan |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Landing | The name, as the `h1` | The line that names her situation | Sign up, then the four points with the scope line last | No card around the hero; the wordmark is not printed above an identical `h1` (G-38) |
| Onboarding step | The question, as an `h1` that takes focus on entry | Its one or two controls | Back | Back exists on every step after the first (G-27) |
| Consent | What is stored | The checkbox and its label | Agree; the notice as a `<details>` in place | G-35, G-47 |
| Home · waiting | PRD order, unchanged: ACOG line · checklist · appointment · links | | | None |
| Home · after the date | The one prompt and "Open My plan" | The appointment block | The rest | None |
| Home · a plan exists | **Her plan, as she entered it, read-only** | The appointment block while a date is ahead; the summary offer when due | Links | G-37, G-32 |
| Questions | The add form | The care-team line | Open questions, then asked ones | As planned |
| Plan | With no plan: the form. With one: the current card or cards | Where two entries differ, and the park action | Replace; then replaced plans, dated | Order was unspecified |
| Meals | Six occasions in the order of her day, each with its meals and one add field | Include in my summary | Delete | As planned |
| Summary | Title; Print at top right, never printed | Three sections | The disclaimer, in the body | Empty: three links, no Print (G-41) |
| Your data | Download | Erase, two-step | Delete account; sign out | Destructive actions never take the filled accent |

- **G-37 · PRD story 4 says "my plan replaces the waiting screen". The plan replaces it with a link to her plan.** From the second week on, the reason she opens this door at a mealtime is to see what her sheet says for that occasion. Home should show it with no tap: the same read-only card the plan page renders, conflicts flagged. No new string is needed; the occasion labels and field labels already exist. *Mechanical: it implements the PRD's sentence. P1.*
- **G-34 · The nav needs a pattern, and the plan gives it a list.** Home, My plan, My meals, My questions, Summary, Your data, plus a persistent "Add a question": at 375 px with 44 px targets that wraps to three rows, about 150 px of chrome above every heading. Decision: row one is the wordmark (it is the Home link) and "Add a question" as the door's one filled action; row two is the four working surfaces in a single wrapping `.chip-row`; "Your data" and the privacy notice sit in the frame's footer on every page. "Add a question" is not rendered on the questions page, where the form is already first. Two rows, about 100 px. *Taste (the arrangement), decided P5; listed at the final gate.*

### Pass 2 — Interaction state coverage · 3/10 → 8/10

The plan specifies success and, for the ask list only, one failure. Everything else is left to the implementer.

| Feature | Loading | Empty | Error | Success | Partial |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Onboarding submit | Button disabled; "Saving" in a polite live region | — | The consent line, or the save-failed line, in `role="alert"`; the form keeps its values | Navigate to Home | Date skipped: stored as null |
| Questions | List region `aria-busy`; the form is usable at once; no skeleton | Rewritten line (G-25) | Load-failed line with retry (G-15) | The item appears first; focus returns to the field; "Saved." is announced | A clinical card *and* the saved item |
| Plan | Same | Rewritten line; the form comes first | Save-failed; **the form keeps every value** (G-26) | The card appears; the form closes | Differ flags; a photo still uploading shows on its own row |
| Meals | Same | One page-level line, only when there are no meals at all. An occasion with none shows its heading and field, not six bleak lines | Same | The meal appears under its occasion | — |
| Summary | Server-rendered; none | The empty line and links to the three lists; **no Print button** (G-41) | The error boundary (G-15) | — | An empty section shows its heading and the empty line |
| Sheet photo | "Saving" on its own row | Just the add control | Cannot decode, or too large: the save-failed line, input cleared (G-20) | A lazy thumbnail | At three photos the add control is not rendered, so a 409 is unreachable |
| Your data | Erase and delete disable in flight | — | Save-failed | Device keys cleared, then the landing | — |

- **G-36 · Four utility strings the states need, one new row.** `gdm-status`: `saving`: Saving · `saved`: Saved. · `removed`: Removed. · `loading`: Loading. Announced through one `aria-live="polite"` region per page (`DESIGN.md` §12). Without them a save that changes nothing visible, such as ticking "Asked" and saving an answer, is silent to everyone and invisible to a screen-reader user. *Mechanical. P1.*
- **G-25 · All four drafted empty states are the one `DESIGN.md` §12 bans by name.** "`"No X found."` alone is banned": warmth, one primary action, context. Drafts, all still `Pending` and all the safety owner's to rule on: questions — "No questions yet. Add one above whenever it comes to you." · plan — "No plan here yet. When your care team gives you one, add it above." · meals — "No meals yet. Start with one you already eat." · summary — "Nothing to print yet. Your plan, meals and questions appear here as you add them." Each passes the plan's own nine copy families. *Mechanical (design-system alignment with an obvious fix). P5.*

### Pass 3 — User journey and emotional arc · 6/10 → 8/10

| # | She does | She feels | Does the plan carry it? |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Taps a link in a forum thread | Frightened, behind, alone | The landing names her situation in her words ✓. The link preview shows another product's name and card ✗ (G-30) |
| 2 | Reads "An organiser. No food guidance." | Deflated. She came for what to eat | Honest ✓. Nothing tells her the waiting screen holds ACOG's published meal structure. Left to the owner and the safety owner; noted, not changed |
| 3 | Sign up → "Prediabetes Pal account … history and coach" | In the wrong room | §8 item 10's default. Final gate |
| 4 | Opens the email link; it opens in another browser | Lost | G-18 |
| 5 | "Have you been told…?" → Yes | Seen | ✓ One question per screen |
| 6 | Date, then consent | Wary at "health data" | Calm copy ✓, unchecked ✓. She cannot go back to fix the date ✗ (G-27). The notice link throws away her place ✗ (G-47) |
| 7 | Home, waiting | There is something to do today | ✓ A checklist with no tick state is the right call |
| 8 | Parks a question at two in the morning | Relief: it is out of her head | ✓ One tap, draft kept. Nothing confirms it to a screen-reader user ✗ (G-36) |
| 9 | Types in her obstetrician's handout | In control | Home forgets her appointment ✗ (G-32) and shows a title instead of her plan ✗ (G-37) |
| 10 | The day before, opens the door | Nervous | No summary offer, because she has a plan ✗ (G-32) |
| 11 | Prints one page and carries it in | Prepared. **The peak** | ✓ Browser print; the disclaimer prints with it |
| 12 | After the birth | Finished | Nothing. Her data stays until she erases it (G-08) |

Five seconds: plain, and it names her. Five minutes: one question parked. Five years: "the thing that got me ready for that appointment." The arc is right. It breaks in the same place three times, steps 9 to 10, which is why G-32 is rated HIGH.

- **G-27 · Onboarding's "back" goes forward, and two steps have no way back at all.** `nextGdmStep("date", "back")` returns `"consent"`: the `date` branch ignores its answer. `nextGdmStep("consent", "back")` returns `"date"`, but `gdm-onboarding-consent` has no `back` string and components may carry no literal, so that branch has no control that can reach it. The browser's Back button leaves `/gdm/start` altogether. Fix: `date` + `back` → `told`; add `back` to the `date` and `consent` rows; test both. *Mechanical. P1.*
- **G-47 · The notice opens in place.** On the consent step the four `gdm-privacy-notice` paragraphs sit in a native `<details>` whose `<summary>` is the existing `notice` string. No navigation, no lost date, no new copy. The full page stays linked from the footer. *Mechanical. P5.*
- **G-35 · The consent button is enabled.** A disabled button takes no focus and explains nothing. Pressing it with the box unticked shows `gdm-consent-required.line`, which is already in the bank, in `role="alert"`, tied to the checkbox with `aria-describedby`. The server refuses an unconsented POST either way. *Mechanical. P5.*

### Pass 4 — AI slop risk · 8/10 → 8/10

Low, and for a good reason: the door is copy-led and plain, which is `DESIGN.md`'s own "document, not dashboard". No hero image, no three-column icon grid, no gradient, no mood copy. None of the seven hard rejections apply to the door pages. The risk runs the other way, toward default HTML: a bulleted list, a card, a button. At D7's "spend nothing" that is acceptable and honest.

Litmus: brand unmistakable in the first screen **yes** · one strong visual anchor **no, and none is proposed** · understandable from headlines alone **yes** · one job per section **yes** · cards necessary **partly** (a plan card is the unit being compared, so yes; the hero card, no) · motion **none, correctly** · holds up with every shadow removed **yes**.

- **G-38 · Three small things on the landing.** (1) The hero sits inside `surface-card`; §11's rule is no card in a hero. Drop the card. (2) Rail 9 puts a marketing CTA at **56 px**, and `/gdm` is marketing by rail 15; the plan's rails list says 44. (3) The frame prints the name as a wordmark directly above an `h1` that is the same name. Move the wordmark into a small component used by `(door)/layout.tsx`, `start` and `privacy`; the landing keeps its `h1`. *Mechanical. P5.*

### Pass 5 — Design system alignment · 7/10 → 9/10

Aligned already: one accent, the radius scale, icons from `components/icons.tsx` only, no dark band, no gamification (§9), errors that say what to do next (§2), health information never in `--text-soft`. To add, each an obvious fix: the 56 px CTA (G-38); §12's empty-state rule (G-25); `role="list"` on any `<ul>` styled without markers, because Safari and VoiceOver drop list semantics otherwise; `break-inside: avoid` on summary rows, where the plan says `.gdm-no-print` and "nothing else"; and one rule the plan implies and never writes down:

- **G-40 · "These differ" never borrows a verdict colour.** In this design system red, amber and green are information (§9). The differ chip is neutral: `--border-strong` on `--surface-muted`, the same on both cards, so neither entry reads as the wrong one. Task 3.4's audit already says "neither styled as the winner"; this gives it a token. *Mechanical.*

### Pass 6 — Responsive and accessibility · 5/10 → 8/10

**Responsive.** One real breakpoint is specified (plan cards side by side from 720 px, which is in the existing 640 / 720 / 880 set) and that is enough, once the nav has a pattern (G-34). Forms are one column at every width. Print is checked at A4 and Letter.

**Accessibility, specified rather than hoped for.** Already in the plan: a skip link, `<main>`, a labelled `<nav>`, labels from the bank, native date input, axe at 375 and 1280, 44 px targets. Missing, and added here:

- **G-39 · Focus goes somewhere on purpose after every action.** A step change moves focus to the new step's `h1` (`tabIndex={-1}`). Adding returns focus to the field. Deleting moves focus to the next item's first control, or to the add field when the list is now empty; today it falls to `<body>`, the classic lost-focus bug. Parking a differ question confirms in place. The six figure inputs sit in one `<fieldset>` whose `<legend>` is the existing `figure` string, each input labelled by its occasion: no new copy. *Mechanical. P1.*
- **G-46 · The clinical card is an alert, and it takes focus.** The plan gives it `role="status"`, which is polite and may be queued behind other speech. For urgent symptoms and possible hypoglycaemia it should be `role="alert"` with focus moved to its heading. Match whatever the first door's result card does for a clinical response; do not invent a second treatment. *Mechanical.*
- **G-44 · HIGH · Her appointment will display as the day before, for every user west of Greenwich.** Task 4.2 says her date is "formatted with `Intl.DateTimeFormat`" and that `today` is "computed in the client component (device-local date)". Neither is specified, and the obvious code for each is UTC: `new Date("2026-10-08")` is midnight UTC, which formats as 7 October in every US timezone; `new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10)` turns into tomorrow each evening, so "tomorrow" reads "today" and `enteredOn` is stamped a day late. `lib/pal/gdm/waiting.ts` does its own arithmetic correctly in UTC; the display and the clock are what is unpinned. Fix: one client helper, `localIsoDate(date)` built from the local getters, and one formatter that constructs from `(y, m - 1, d)`; a test for each with a late-evening `Date`. *Mechanical. P1. Carried into Phase 3.*

A `/gdm/signin` page and second email (shared auth; final gate). A visual anchor or illustration for the landing (D7: spend nothing). Any celebration on a first question or a printed summary (§9 and F-CALM-GDM forbid it, correctly). Dark mode. A post-implementation `/design-review` pass, which should happen once PR-2 renders.

### Step 0 — Scope challenge, against the code

**What the plan claims about the tree, checked.**

| Claim | Verdict |
|---|---|
| `ledgerStatuses` is exported, pure, and returns what the new guard indexes into | **Verified.** `Map<string, LedgerRowState[]>`, `LedgerRowState = { status, active }` (`lib/guide-door-guard.ts:262, 278`). It reads *every* table with those headers; `getCopyLedgerRows` reads the first. The plan's one-table filing rule keeps the two in step, and the parity test makes a slip loud |
| `SessionInfo` admits `null`, so `getSession?: () => Promise<SessionInfo>` compiles with a stub that returns null | **Verified** (`lib/server/session.ts:6`) |
| PGlite tests can see the new tables | **Verified, with an ordering consequence the plan already respects.** `createTestDb()` builds from `drizzle/*.sql`, not from `schema.ts`. Task 1.1 generates the migration (Step 4) before its first passing run (Step 6) |
| The next migration is `0020`; `--name gdm-door` matches the naming style | **Verified** (`0019_profile-orientation.sql` is the latest) |
| zod has `z.partialRecord` | **Verified** (4.4.3) |
| A listed surface with the twin unset fails the production build | **Verified, and that is G-09** (`next.config.ts:49-74` for the precedent) |
| The ledger reaches a new route's function bundle | **Verified as low risk.** There is no `outputFileTracingIncludes` anywhere; `loadSafetyContract()` reads five files under `process.cwd()` and is traced through the import graph, the way `/api/check` already gets them. Task 2.3's preview check stands, and G-11 makes any failure loud on the first ordinary question. Confidence 7/10 |
| The drafted Tier 1 strings clear the existing claims audit | **Verified by inspection against all nineteen families** (`claims-boundary-copy.test.ts:124-258`). The author's own catch ("Diagnosed" → "Told") was the only collision. Source files have whole comment lines stripped; a *trailing* comment is scanned, so an executor's `// we manage the draft here` would trip `product-agent-verb` |
| Existing directory-walking tests will not go red on new GDM files | **Verified.** Eight tests walk `app`, `components` or `lib`. None classifies pages or pins storage keys in a way the door trips. `seo-meta` needs no entry per page; the two the plan adds are belt and braces |
| Every line number quoted in the plan | **Treat as a hint.** The ledger and `globals.css` change weekly. Anchor on the named symbol |

**Complexity check.** Trips it (about 45 files), and is the right size for a new surface with its own consent path. No reduction. **Search check.** Native date input, browser print, `<details>`, and zod's own `partialRecord` are all the platform's built-ins, chosen over libraries. Nothing here rolls a custom solution where a built-in exists. **[Layer 1]** throughout. **Completeness check.** The plan is the complete version almost everywhere; its shortcuts are the two-call Replace (G-13) and four copies of the route guard (G-16), both reversed above.

### Section 1 — Architecture (delta over Phase 1)

The diagram in Phase 1 §1 stands. Two additions.

- **G-28 · The dark-door smoke cannot see the leak it was written to catch.** Task L.2 asserts a dark `/gdm/home` is a 404 whose body contains neither the working name nor `/gestational/i`. `(door)/layout.tsx` "renders `<GdmNav />` above `{children}` — nothing else", with no flag check, and the nav's strings ("My plan", "My questions") contain neither word. If Next ever renders that segment layout around a not-found, the door's navigation is on screen before its class exists and the test stays green. Add the one-line flag check the outer layout already has, and assert that a `gdm-nav` string is absent. Also worth a comment in the spec: **never add a `loading.tsx` under `app/gdm/`**; a streamed shell turns the dark 404 into a 200. `(confidence 7/10 on Next's behaviour, 9/10 on the assertion gap)` *Mechanical. P1.*
- **G-29 · Tier 2: a vague description must not read as "about".** §5.3 compares the model's estimate *range* with her figure, and "about" means the two overlap. A description with no portions ("some rice and curry") honestly yields a wide range, say 20 to 90 g, and a wide range overlaps almost any figure. So the vaguer she is, the likelier she is told "About what your plan says", which is the one reassuring state of the three. The plan leaves the escape to the model's own `unclear`. Make it arithmetic, in `compareToPlan`: when the estimate is wider than the band it is being compared with (for a single figure, wider than twice `ABOUT_TOLERANCE` of it; for a typed range, wider than that range), return `unclear` with `missing: "portion"`, never `about`. Add to Task G3.1's test list beside the symmetry case, and to the fixed panel as one case per unit. Like `ABOUT_TOLERANCE` itself this is **[A]** and belongs in S1's Verdict Semantics entry. `(8/10)` *Mechanical. P1.*
- **G-50 · Tier 2's model client sits outside the repo's only privacy pin.** `privacy-minimal.test.ts` asserts `store: false` on `lib/pal/openai-client.ts` and nowhere else. `plan-read-model.ts` builds its own request. The plan says in prose that it uses `store: false`; Task G3.2's test list does not. Add two source pins: `store:\s*false` in the new client, and no `console.*` in `app/api/gdm/read/route.ts`. `(9/10)` *Mechanical. P1.*

### Section 2 — Code quality

- **G-17 · `gdm-copy.test.ts`'s "no literal copy" scan is loose in both directions.** `/(?<!=)>\s*[A-Za-z][^<>{}]{2,}</` reads `days > limit && <Row />` as JSX text (a false alarm the first time a component compares two identifiers). It does not see `{"Saving"}`, `{saving ? "Saving" : label}`, `aria-label={'Close'}` or a template literal, which is how a literal actually arrives. A guard that cries wolf gets loosened by whoever is on deadline. TypeScript is already a dependency: walk each file's AST and flag a non-blank `JsxText`, and any string literal inside a `JsxExpression` or a `JsxAttribute` whose name is not on a short allowlist (`className`, `href`, `id`, `type`, `role`, `htmlFor`, `inputMode`, `accept`, `capture`, `name`, `rel`, `target`, `key`, `autoComplete`). About twenty-five lines, no false alarms, and the four bypasses close. `(9/10 on the misses, 8/10 on the false alarm)` *Mechanical. P5.*
- **G-23 · `before_appointment` compares strings that may not be dates.** Task 6.2: `"yes"` when `today <= appointmentDate`. When the date fails to decrypt it is the literal `(unreadable entry)`, and `"("` sorts before every digit, so the event reports `no` where it should report `no_date`. Reuse `waiting.ts`'s parse. `(9/10)` *Mechanical.*
- **G-51 · Tasks 1.1 to 1.3 are one commit; say it once.** The plan says it twice, differently (Task 1.1 Step 6: "commit 1.1–1.3 together, or land 1.3's `COVERAGE` lines with this commit"; Task 1.2 Step 5: "with Task 1.1 and 1.3, or alone if 1.3 has landed"). `account-export`'s schema-derived denominator is red from the moment the tables exist until the export covers them. One instruction: no commit until 1.3 is green. *Mechanical. P5.*
- **G-21, G-16, G-22, G-13** from Phase 1 stand.
