```
  └── unreadable row is counted              [GAP]  G-12            ├── [GAP]   a wide estimate is `unclear`        G-29
[+] routeAboveFifth                           [★★★]                  └── [GAP]   store:false + no logging pins       G-50
[+] plan-record incl. no-arithmetic pin       [★★★]
[+] plan-photo route                          [★★★]
  └── Content-Length guard; decode failure   [GAP]  G-20
[+] waiting.ts                                [★★★]
  └── localIsoDate, local display            [GAP]  G-44
[+] meals: schema, grouping, copy             [★★★]

    labels, only punctuation is left")        [★★★]                QUALITY   ★★★ 22 · ★★ 4 · ★ 1
[+] summary page: before_appointment          [GAP]  G-23          GAPS      30 (5 E2E flows, 2 eval-side, 1 bug)
[+] kill-lines doc test                       [★★ ]
```

**Reading it.** The unit layer is strong, and in places unusually good: the summary builder's test, and a negative control for every regex family. The flow layer is nearly empty, because without a DOM every component is tested by reading its source. **G-03 is therefore the most valuable test in this review:** one signed-in Playwright walk (sign in, consent box unticked, consent, park a question, a shaky message raises the alert, two differing plans, summary renders, erase, the door forgets her). It converts five of the flow gaps from a checklist into a check, and it is the 2 a.m. Friday test. The repo already has the signed-in smoke pattern from PR-5.

**Regression rule.** No existing behaviour is modified, so no regression test is owed. The flag-off CI leg is the regression proof for the first door, and its name is preserved as the required check. One caution: Task 0.2 hoists `readIfPresent` out of the guide-door block in `next.config.ts`; `next-config-twin-guard.test.ts` and `flag-server-twins.test.ts` already cover that block, so the hoist is pinned.

**Test plan artifact:** `~/.gstack/projects/revora/tefera-main-eng-review-test-plan-20260920.md`.

### Section 4 — Performance

Nothing new beyond Phase 1 §7. One measurement to take rather than assume: an account export that carries three sheet photos is roughly 8–9 MB of JSON built in memory and returned in one response. Verify it once on a preview with three maximum-size photos (Task 3.3, a new Step 6). If it fails, the export omits photo bytes and lists each photo's id and size instead, and "Your data" links each photo for download. `(6/10: platform limits have moved; measure, do not guess)` *Accepted as a verification step, G-62.*

### Executor and operator experience (in place of Phase 3.5; see G-61)

- **G-52 · HIGH · Execution cannot start from this tree, and one command would make a mess of it.** The plan and the PRD it implements are both **untracked**, so an executor in a fresh worktree, which the plan's own header recommends, will not find either. The tree also holds 60 untracked files, 51 of them under `docs/`, and six modified ones including `copy-ledger.md` and `DESIGN.md`. Task 1.3 Step 5 runs `git add app/api/gdm app/api/account lib tests docs drizzle`, which would sweep all 51 unrelated documents into a GDM commit, and any commit of the ledger would carry the owner's uncommitted edits. Fix: a Task 0.0 (commit the plan and both PRDs on a docs branch, as the guide plan was; start from a clean worktree), and every `git add` names files, never `lib`, `tests` or `docs`. `(10/10: counted)` *Mechanical. P1.*
- **G-49 · `npm run test:pal -- gdm-door` does not run one file. It runs seventy-four.** `test:pal` is `vitest run tests/unit/pal`; the appended name is a second filter, and vitest runs a file that matches *any* filter. Measured: 74 files with the name, 74 without. The plan uses this form about forty-five times, so every red-green step pays for the whole suite, and an "Expected: PASS" can be failed by a test that has nothing to do with the task. Replace each with `npx vitest run tests/unit/pal/<file>.test.ts`, the form the plan already uses outside `pal/`. Keep `npm run test:pal` for the end-of-task runs. `(10/10: measured)` *Mechanical. P5.*
- **G-60 · The gates file is edited by someone who is not a developer; the error should tell them what it wants.** `gateStatuses` accepts exactly `Signed`. A decider who types `signed`, `Signed ✅` or `Signed 2026-10-01` gets "needs gate S1, which reads "signed" … only its decider signs it", which tells a person who *did* sign it nothing. Add "(the cell must read exactly `Signed`)" to the message and a test for the three near-misses. *Mechanical. P1.*
- **G-57 · KL-T1's A arm has an exact floor, and §14 should use it.** A is parked ÷ opened, read from Umami "unique visitors". Umami's visitor is a hash that includes the IP, so a phone moving between Wi-Fi and mobile data is several visitors over four weeks, and most of those visits open the list without parking: the ratio reads low, which biases the line toward firing. The database gives a bound for free. Everyone who opened the list is a door user, so **(distinct users with an `ask` row) ÷ (door users) can only understate A**. If that exact figure is 25% or more, A has not fired, whatever Umami says. Only when it is below 25% does the Umami estimate matter. One paragraph in §14.1. `(10/10 on the bound, 6/10 on Umami's internals)` *Mechanical. P1.*
- **G-61 · Phase 3.5 (DX) is not run as a separate eight-pass review.** The devex skill's own applicability gate asks for a developer-facing product: an API, CLI, SDK, platform or docs that developers install or build on. This is a consumer health app; its routes are internal and its only "developers" are the executor and the owner. The 2026-09-14 review did score this repo's internal toolchain once ("product type: internal"); the toolchain has not changed, so repeating the scorecard would restate it. What is specific to *this* plan is above: G-49, G-51, G-52, G-57, G-60. *Process decision. P3. Listed at the final gate so the owner can ask for the full pass.*

## Cross-phase themes

A concern that surfaced in two or more phases. With one reviewer these are not independent voices agreeing; they are the same weakness showing up from different angles, which is still the strongest signal this record has.

1. **The plan specifies what is said, and not what happens.** Copy is governed to the word: banked, ledgered, parity-tested. The behaviour of the screen is nearly unspecified. *Phase 1 §4* (no in-flight state on any form; three errors that give untrue advice), *Phase 2 Pass 2* (states 3/10), *Phase 3 §3* (user flows 14% covered). G-14, G-15, G-26, G-36, G-39.
2. **Each safety mechanism is tested where it cannot fail.** The revert is rehearsed on a preview, where the guard that blocks it never runs (G-09). The dark-door smoke looks for two words the leak would not contain (G-28). The literal-copy scan misses the way a literal actually arrives (G-17). The build checks what a gate says, not who said it (G-31). The consent default is checked by a person with a list (G-03). Every guard is real. Each is pointed at a case it would pass anyway. *Phases 1 and 3.*
3. **The first door leaks into the second at the edges nobody looked at.** Link preview, home-screen name and the installed app's start URL (G-30); an erase that covers both doors (G-05); a sign-in page describing "history and coach" (G-48); a shared sign-in that loses in-app-browser visitors (G-18). *Phases 1 and 2.* The isolation of the door's own directories is excellent. The leaks are all in what it inherits.
4. **Everything after "a plan is entered" is thin.** The appointment vanishes (G-32); Home shows a link where the PRD says her plan (G-37); Replace is two calls with no recovery (G-13); a plan can never be corrected, only replaced. *Phases 1 and 2.* The waiting weeks are designed; the weeks after are not.
5. **Distribution is the half of the plan that was not written.** Premise P2, the unread rule (G-02), the funnel read (G-04), in-app browsers (G-18), the link preview (G-30), the measurement bias (G-57). *Phases 1 and 3.* Every kill line assumes fifty arrivals, and the only sentence about arrivals is "post the link in one place".

## Post-gate addendum (2026-09-20, written after the final gate)

**What happened.** Four subagents were launched: an independent CEO voice, an independent design voice (on Opus), and two code verifiers. All four finished within fifteen minutes, before either gate. Their reports were delivered only when the primary reviewer's turn ended, which was after the owner had approved. The owner therefore ruled twice on one reviewer's analysis. This addendum is what the other three said. Nothing in it has been applied unless marked **applied**.

### Independently confirmed (the strongest findings in this record)

| Finding | Confirmed by |
|---|---|
| G-30, the first door's brand in the tab, the link preview and the manifest | `verify-data-routes`, which called it its biggest finding: "the plan's page-text unit test goes green while the shipped page shows Prediabetes Pal" |
| G-49, `npm run test:pal -- name` runs everything | `verify-config-copy`, measured separately: 74 files with the name, 74 without |
| G-32, entering a plan strands her | The design voice's **first** finding, rated critical: "Home strands her the moment the product works" |
| G-27, onboarding's Back goes forward | The design voice, critical: "Onboarding Back is a lie", plus the two missing `back` strings |
| G-25 empty states · G-34 no nav pattern · G-46 the clinical card is announced politely · G-06 the holding screen dead-ends | The design voice, each independently |
| The ledger reaches new routes through the import graph; no tracing config exists | `verify-config-copy`, with a caveat worth keeping: `npm run e2e` starts from the repo root where `docs/` exists, so **a green e2e can never detect a tracing gap**. Only Task 2.3's preview check can |
| Tier 1 drafted strings clear the claims audit | `verify-config-copy`: nineteen families × 25 rows, zero hits. It noted the Tier 2 banks were unscanned; scanned since, 26 strings, zero hits |

### Caught by the voices and missed by the primary review

- **G-72 · USER CHALLENGE · The plan's deciders may not exist.** The plan's spine is that gates are opened by "a decider who is not the executor": the safety owner, and counsel where a claim class is created. Phase 1 took that as given and asked only how long the review would take (premise P1). The CEO voice asked **who** it is, and the primary reviewer has since verified every fact it cited:
  - `docs/safety/claims-boundary.md` has four commits in its history, by `tkiros` (1) and `Codex`, an agent (3). All ten existing claim classes were created in those commits. `DESIGN.md` §1.3 says "Creating a class is a decision for counsel"; the repository shows no sign it has ever been made that way.
  - Every commit that has touched the copy ledger, where rows are flipped to `Approved`, is by `tkiros` (49) or `Codex` (24).
  - `docs/qa/dietitian-review/clinical-copy-governance.json` reads `"clinicalApprovalStatus": "pending_external_panel"` and every `clinicalApproverIds` array is empty. The ledger's own approved row `about-page-review-status` says: "No external clinician has signed off on Prediabetes Pal's guidance copy."
  - The guide plan's §1 shows 40 rows filed `Pending` across three batches, a submission drafted 2026-09-17 and not sent, and its own small demand test (D5) "Not started — rules page unread, draft unsent."

  **What this does and does not show.** A git log cannot see a lawyer's email or a dietitian's phone call, and "safety owner" may be a role the owner holds on purpose. What the repository shows is that no approval has ever been *recorded* by anyone but the founder or an agent, and that the one external review already commissioned has produced nothing in four months. If the deciders are the owner, the gates still do real work: they separate an AI executor from a human decision. But then S3 (a reviewer with pregnancy competence) is the only gate that *requires* an outsider, its twin has never closed, and §1 should say in plain words who certifies copy for pregnant women. Both reviewers now hold this view. It is the owner's to rule on.
- **The `result.error` test shape (verifier A6). Applied.** The twin-guard test file has no `loadConfig` at all, and three different helper shapes exist across three files. The snippets in Task 0.2, including the one this review had written for G-09, would not have compiled. Rewritten against the real helpers.
- **G-64 · `npm run contract` proves nothing about a `Pending` row's class.** It checks the class only on rows that are `Approved` and `Active`. A `Pending` row naming `gdm-organiser` passes green, and nobody learns the class does not exist until the production guard refuses the flip. The plan says this in Global Constraints; the executor needs it in Task 0.4. **Applied** as a one-line note.
- **G-65 · `privacy-stateful.test.ts` scans the whole text of `schema.ts`** for a quoted `a1c` or `food`, not column names. The four `kind` values are fine. A future kind named `'food'` would fail it. **Applied** as a one-line trap note in Task 1.1.
- **G-66 · Tier 2 banks were never scanned against the claims audit.** Done for the 26 strings drafted in §5 (clean). The S3 reviewer's entries will be new text, so the same scan is owed before Task G1.1 lands. **Applied** to the §5 block.
- **G-67 · The `/api/health` comment the twin rule cites is already untrue.** It says a flag may skip its twin "only when the door adds no server boundary"; `guide-door-flag.ts:11-22` records that `orient` adds one. Task 0.3 should correct the comment rather than inherit it. **Applied** as a note.
- **G-71 · An abandon date for the reviewer queue** (CEO voice). Send the guide door's existing submission first. If nothing in that queue is approved within four weeks, this door should not be started. Proposed as a §1 row beside the reviewer row the owner added at the premise gate. **Not applied: the owner's call.**

### New from the design voice, awaiting the owner

- **G-68 · The landing sells before it scopes.** Order today: name, hero line, **Sign up**, then four points with "An organiser. No food guidance." last. She signs up before learning the door gives no food guidance, which is the largest expectation trap here and inflates the denominators KL-T1 is read on. Move the scope line directly under the hero line, above the CTA. *Recommended.*
- **G-69 · Home should lead with her appointment, not with ACOG's sentence.** The primary review kept PRD order (ACOG line, checklist, appointment, links). The design voice: her date and her next action should lead; the quoted meal structure is the riskiest content on the door and is still open under D5, so "building it as the visual lead means a refusal guts the screen." The primary reviewer is persuaded, for a further reason: G-32 made the appointment block a fixture of all three states, and a fixture belongs in one fixed place. Order (c) → (b) → (a) → (d). *Recommended; it reads the PRD's list as a list, not as a layout.*
- **G-70 · Meals is six forms.** Six `<h2>` sections, each with its own field, Save and checkbox, is a wall at 375 px. One add form with a native `<select>` for the occasion; render an occasion's section only once it holds a meal. Still grouped the way her day runs. *Recommended; supersedes half of G-25's meals note.*
- Also from that voice, folded into existing findings without a new id: focus targets for the two-step erase confirm and for the nav's `#add` jump (G-39); place the clinical card next to what she just typed (G-46); give the holding screen one way out (G-06). **Rejected:** "Step 2 of 3" on onboarding. It is a digit in a bank string, and `DESIGN.md` §13 lists `Step N` eyebrows as a confirmed anti-pattern.
- **Findings 11 to 14 arrived at 06:39Z, after the addendum gate.** Already covered: the doubled door name (G-38), the disabled consent button (G-35), in-flight and success states on the plan form (G-26, G-36), and the two-current-plans state, which G-13 removes rather than explains. **Three were new and mechanical, so they were applied and logged without a third question to the owner, who may strike any of them:** Replace opens prefilled with her own previous values (G-75; the voice read the `defaultValue` pin as banning this, but that regex bans only a digit literal); deleting a question or a meal takes two presses (G-76); Your data is reordered and its destructive pair de-weighted (G-77). **One is left to the owner because it contradicts the PRD:** the voice wants a `pal.gdm.plan.draft` key, since a phone that evicts a backgrounded tab loses eleven typed fields. The plan and PRD §7.4 store nothing health-related on the device, and a figure is health data. G-26 keeps the form in memory through a failed save; it does not survive an evicted tab.
- **Its litmus answers, against the primary review's:** 1 PARTIAL (primary: YES; "the h1 is a category label, the promise sits in body text") · 2 PARTIAL · 3 NO · 4 NO · 5 NO · 6 PARTIAL · 7 NO. **Six of seven confirmed, one disagreement.** Unlike the CEO voice, the design voice and the primary review reached nearly the same verdict independently.

One row per decision, appended as it was made. **Classification:** *Mechanical* = one clearly right answer, decided silently · *Taste* = reasonable people could disagree, surfaced at the final gate · *Owner* = needs the owner because it reads a PRD or D-decision differently than written. **Principles:** P1 completeness · P2 boil the lake (blast radius, under a day) · P3 pragmatic · P4 DRY · P5 explicit over clever · P6 bias to action.
